\displaystyle\mathsf{Adv}_{\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{ot}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ind}\mbox{-}\mathsf{kla}}(1^{\lambda})\coloneqq

(31)

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\Pr[\expc{\SKECRSKL,\qA}{ot}{ind}{kla} (1^\secp,0) \ra 1] - \Pr[\expc{\SKECRSKL,\qA}{ot}{ind}{kla} (1^\secp,1) \ra 1] }\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

(32)

5
SKE-CR-SKL with Key Testability

In this section, we show how to achieve SKE-CR-SKL introduced in
Section

4.2
.

5.1
Construction

We construct an SKE-CR-SKL scheme with key testability
\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}=\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.(\mathsf{Setup},\mathpzc{KG},\mathsf{Enc},\mathpzc{Dec},\allowbreak\mathpzc{Vrfy})
having the
additional algorithms
\mathsf{CDec}
and
\mathsf{KeyTest}
, using the
following building blocks.

•

BB84-based SKE-CD scheme (Definition
3.12
)
\mathsf{SKECD}=\mathsf{SKECD}.(\mathsf{KG},\mathpzc{Enc},\mathpzc{Dec},\allowbreak\mathpzc{Del},\mathsf{Vrfy})
having the classical
decryption algorithm
\mathsf{SKECD}.\mathsf{CDec}
.

•

OWF
f:\{0,1\}^{\lambda}\rightarrow\{0,1\}^{p(\lambda)}
for some polynomial
p
.

Let
\mathcal{M}\coloneqq\{0,1\}^{\ell_{\mathsf{m}}}
be the plaintext space. The construction is as follows:

\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
:

1.

Generate
r\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{\ell_{\mathsf{m}}}
.

2.

Generate
\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk}\leftarrow\mathsf{SKECD}.\mathsf{KG}(1^{\lambda})
.

3.

Output
\mathsf{msk}\coloneqq(\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk},r)
.

\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{KG}(\mathsf{msk})
:

1.

Parse
\mathsf{msk}=(\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk},r)
.

2.

Generate

(\mathsf{skecd}.\mathpzc{ct},\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{vk})\leftarrow\mathsf{SKECD}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk},r)
.

\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{vk}
is of the form

(x,\theta)\in\{0,1\}^{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}}\times\{0,1\}^{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}}
, and

\mathsf{skecd}.\mathpzc{ct}
is of the form

\ket{\psi_1}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{1}}}}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\ket{\psi_{\ctlen}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}}}}}}
.

3.

Generate
s_{i,b}\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{\lambda}
and compute
t_{i,b}\leftarrow f(s_{i,b})
for every
i\in[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]
and
b\in\{0,1\}
.
Set
T\coloneqq t_{1,0}\|t_{1,1}\|\cdots\|t_{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}},0}\|t_{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}},1}
and
S=\{s_{i,0}\oplus s_{i,1}\}_{i\in[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]\;:\;\theta[i]=1}
.

4.

Prepare a register
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{i}}}}
that is initialized to

\ket{0^\secp}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{i}}}}}
for every
i\in[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]
.

5.

For every
i\in[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]
, apply the map

\displaystyle\ket{u_i}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{i}}}}}\otimes\ket{v_i}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{i}}}}}\rightarrow\ket{u_i}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{i}}}}}\otimes\ket{v_i\oplus s_{i,u_i}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{i}}}}}

(33)

to the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{i}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{i}}}}
and obtain the resulting state
\rho_{i}
.

6.

Output
\mathpzc{dk}=(\rho_{i})_{i\in{[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]}}
,

\mathsf{vk}=(x,\theta,S)
, and
\mathsf{tk}=T
.

\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{msk},\mathsf{m})
:

1.

Parse
\mathsf{msk}=(\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk},r)
.

2.

Output
\mathsf{ct}\coloneqq(\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk},r\oplus\mathsf{m})
.

\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{CDec}(\mathsf{dk},\mathsf{ct})
:

1.

Parse
\mathsf{ct}=(\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk},z)
. Let

\widetilde{\mathsf{dk}}
be the sub-string of
\mathsf{dk}
on register

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT}}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{1}}}}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}}}}}
.

2.

Output
z\oplus\mathsf{SKECD}.\mathsf{CDec}(\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk},\widetilde{\mathsf{dk}})
.

\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{Dec}(\mathpzc{dk},\mathsf{ct})
:

1.

Parse
(\rho_{i})_{i\in[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]}
. We denote the register
holding
\rho_{i}
as
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{i}}}}\otimes{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{i}}}}
for
every
i\in[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]
.

2.

Prepare a register
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{MSG}}}
of
\ell_{\mathsf{m}}
qubits that is
initialized to
\ket{0\cdots 0}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{MSG}}}}
.

3.

Apply the map

\displaystyle\ket{u}_{\bigotimes_{i\in[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{i}}}}}\otimes\ket{w}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{MSG}}}}\rightarrow

(34)

\displaystyle\ket{u}_{\bigotimes_{i\in[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{i}}}}}\otimes\ket{w\oplus\SKECRSKL.\CDec(u, \ct)}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{MSG}}}}

(35)

to the registers

\bigotimes_{i\in[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{i}}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{MSG}}}
.

4.

Measure
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{MSG}}}
in the computational basis and output the result
\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
.

\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\widetilde{\mathpzc{dk}})
:

1.

Parse
\mathsf{vk}=(x,\theta,S=\{s_{i,0}\oplus s_{i,1}\}_{i\in[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]\;:\;\theta[i]=1})
and

\mathpzc{dk}=(\rho_{i})_{i\in[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]}
where
\rho_{i}
is a state on
the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{i}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{i}}}}
.

2.

For every
i\in[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]
, measure
\rho_{i}
in the Hadamard
basis to get outcomes
c_{i},d_{i}
corresponding to
the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{i}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{i}}}}
respectively.

3.

Output
\top
if
x[i]=c_{i}\oplus d_{i}\cdot(s_{i,0}\oplus s_{i,1})
holds for every
i\in[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]
such that
\theta[i]=1
.
Otherwise, output
\bot
.

\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{KeyTest}(\mathsf{tk},\mathsf{dk})
:

1.

Parse
\mathsf{dk}
as a string over the registers

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT}}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{1}}}}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S}}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{1}}}}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}}}}}
.
Let
u_{i}
denote the value on

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{i}}}}
and
v_{i}
the value on
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{i}}}}
. Parse
\mathsf{tk}

as
T=t_{1,0}\|t_{1,1}\|\cdots\|t_{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}},0}\|t_{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}},1}
.

2.

Let
\mathsf{Check}[t_{i,0},t_{i_{1}}](u_{i},v_{i})
be the deterministic
algorithm that outputs
1
if
f(v_{i})=t_{i,u_{i}}
holds and
0

otherwise.

3.

Output
\mathsf{Check}[t_{1,0},t_{1,1}](u_{1},v_{1})\land\cdots\land\mathsf{Check}[t_{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}},0},t_{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}},1}](u_{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}},v_{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}})
.

Decryption correctness:

For a ciphertext
\mathsf{ct}=(\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk},r\oplus\mathsf{m})
, the decryption algorithm
\mathpzc{Dec}
performs
the following computation:

\displaystyle\ket{u}_{\bigotimes_{i\in[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{i}}}}}\otimes\ket{w}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{MSG}}}}\rightarrow

(36)

\displaystyle\ket{u}_{\bigotimes_{i\in[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{i}}}}}\otimes\ket{w
\xor r \xor\msg\xor\SKECD.\CDec(\skecd.\sk, u)}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{MSG}}}}

(37)

Recall that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT}}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{1}}}}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}}}}}
is a register corresponding to the
ciphertext of a BB84-based SKE-CD scheme
\mathsf{SKECD}
. Hence, from the Classical
Decryption property of
\mathsf{SKECD}
(Definition
3.12
), it must be that

\mathsf{SKECD}.\mathsf{CDec}(\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk},u)=r
for every
u
in the superposition
of
\mathsf{skecd}.\mathpzc{ct}
. Consequently,
\mathsf{m}
is written onto the

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{MSG}}}
register in each term of the superposition and decryption
correctness follows.

Verification correctness:

Observe that for the Hadamard basis positions (
i\in[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]
such that

\theta[i]=1
),
\rho_{i}
is of the form:

\rho[i]=\ket{0}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{i}}}}}\ket{s_{i,0}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{i}}}}}+(-1)^{x[i]}\ket{1}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{i}}}}}\ket{s_{i,1}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{i}}}}}

It is easy to see that measuring the state in the Hadamard basis gives
outcomes
c_{i},d_{i}
(on registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{i}}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{i}}}}
respectively) satisfying
x[i]=c_{i}\oplus d_{i}\cdot(s_{i,0}\oplus s_{i,1})
. Hence, the verification correctness follows.

Theorem 5.1.

There exists an SKE-CR-SKL scheme satisfying
OT-IND-KLA security and Key-Testability, assuming the existence of a
BB84-based SKE-CD scheme and the existence of an OWF.

We prove this theorem in the subsequent sections.

5.2
Proof of OT-IND-KLA Security

Let
\mathpzc{A}
be an adversary for the OT-IND-KLA security of the
construction
\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}
that makes use of a BB84-based
SKE-CD scheme
\mathsf{SKECD}
. Consider the hybrid
\mathsf{Hyb}_{j}^{\mathsf{coin}}
defined
as follows:

\mathsf{Hyb}_{j}^{\mathsf{coin}}
:

1.

The challenger
\mathpzc{Ch}
runs
\mathsf{msk}\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})

and initializes
\mathpzc{A}
with input
1^{\lambda}
.

2.

\mathpzc{A}
requests
q
decryption keys for some polynomial

q
. For each
k\in[j]
,
\mathpzc{Ch}
generates

(\mathpzc{dk}_{i},\mathsf{vk}_{i},\mathsf{tk}_{i})\leftarrow\mathpzc{\widetilde{KG}}(\mathsf{msk})
where
\mathpzc{\widetilde{KG}}
is defined
as follows (the difference from
\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{KG}
is colored
in red):

\mathpzc{\widetilde{KG}}(\mathsf{msk})
:

(a)

Parse
\mathsf{msk}=(\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk},r)
.

(b)

Sample
\widetilde{r}\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{\ell_{\mathsf{m}}}
.

(c)

Generate

(\mathsf{skecd}.\mathpzc{ct},\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{vk})\leftarrow\mathsf{SKECD}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\widetilde{r}})
.

\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{vk}
is of the form

(x,\theta)\in\{0,1\}^{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}}\times\{0,1\}^{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}}
, and
\mathsf{skecd}.\mathpzc{ct}

can be described as

\ket{\psi_1}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{1}}}}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\ket{\psi_{\ctlen}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}}}}}}
.

(d)

Generate
s_{i,b}\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{\lambda}
and compute
t_{i,b}\leftarrow f(s_{i,b})
for every
i\in[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]
and
b\in\{0,1\}
.
Set
T\coloneqq t_{1,0}\|t_{1,1}\|\cdots\|t_{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}},0}\|t_{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}},1}
and
S=\{s_{i,0}\oplus s_{i,1}\}_{i\in[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]\;:\;\theta[i]=1}
.

(e)

Prepare a register
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{i}}}}
that is initialized to
\ket{0\cdots 0}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{i}}}}}
for every
i\in[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]
.

(f)

For every
i\in[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]
, apply the map

\displaystyle\ket{u_i}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{i}}}}}\otimes\ket{v_i}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{i}}}}}\rightarrow\ket{u_i}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{i}}}}}\otimes\ket{v_i\oplus s_{i,u_i}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{1}}}}}

(38)

to the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{i}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{i}}}}
and obtain the resulting state
\rho_{i}
.

(g)

Output
\mathpzc{dk}=(\rho_{i})_{i\in[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]}
,
\mathsf{vk}=(x,\theta,S)
, and
\mathsf{tk}=T
.

3.

On the other hand, for
k=j+1,\ldots,q
,
\mathpzc{Ch}
generates

(\mathpzc{dk}_{k},\mathsf{vk}_{k},\mathsf{tk}_{k})\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{KG}(\mathsf{msk})
. Then,

\mathpzc{Ch}
sends
(\mathpzc{dk}_{k},\mathsf{tk}_{k})_{k\in[q]}
to
\mathpzc{A}
.

4.

\mathpzc{A}
can
get access to the following (stateful) verification oracle

O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{Vrfy}}}
where
V_{i}
is initialized to
\bot
:

O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{Vrfy}}}(i,\widetilde{\mathpzc{dk}})
:

It runs
d\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk}_{i},\widetilde{\mathpzc{dk}})
and returns
d
.
If
V_{i}=\bot
and
d=\top
, it updates
V_{i}\coloneqq\top
.

5.

\mathpzc{A}
sends
(\mathsf{m}_{0}^{*},\mathsf{m}_{1}^{*})\in\{0,1\}^{\ell_{\mathsf{m}}}\times\{0,1\}^{\ell_{\mathsf{m}}}
to
\mathpzc{Ch}
. If
V_{i}=\bot
for
some
i\in[q]
,
\mathpzc{Ch}
outputs
0
as the final output of this
experiment. Otherwise,
\mathpzc{Ch}
generates

\mathsf{ct}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{msk},\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{coin}}^{*})
and sends
\mathsf{ct}^{*}
to

\mathpzc{A}
.

6.

\mathpzc{A}
outputs a guess
\mathsf{coin}^{\prime}
for
\mathsf{coin}
. The
challenger outputs
\mathsf{coin}^{\prime}
as the final output of the
experiment.

We will now prove the following lemma:

Lemma 5.2.

\forall j\in\{0,\ldots,q-1\}
and
\mathsf{coin}\in\{0,1\}:\mathsf{Hyb}_{j}^{\mathsf{coin}}\approx\mathsf{Hyb}_{j+1}^{\mathsf{coin}}
.

Proof.

Suppose
\mathsf{Hyb}_{j}^{\mathsf{coin}}\not\approx\mathsf{Hyb}_{j+1}^{\mathsf{coin}}
. Let
\mathpzc{D}
be
a corresponding distinguisher. We will
construct a reduction
\mathpzc{R}
that breaks the IND-CVA-CD security of
the BB84-based SKE-CD scheme
\mathsf{SKECD}
. The execution of
\mathpzc{R}{D}
in
the experiment
\mathsf{Exp}_{\mathsf{SKECD},\mathpzc{R}}^{\mathsf{ind}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cva}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cd}}(1^{\lambda},b)
proceeds
as follows:

Execution of
\mathpzc{R}{D}
in

\mathsf{Exp}_{\mathsf{SKECD},\mathpzc{R}}^{\mathsf{ind}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cva}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cd}}(1^{\lambda},b)
:

1.

The challenger
\mathpzc{Ch}
computes
\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk}\leftarrow\mathsf{SKECD}.\mathsf{KG}(1^{\lambda})
.

2.

\mathpzc{R}
samples
(r_{0},r_{1})\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{\ell_{\mathsf{m}}}\times\{0,1\}^{\ell_{\mathsf{m}}}
and
sends it to
\mathpzc{Ch}
.

3.

\mathpzc{Ch}
computes
(\mathsf{skecd}.\mathpzc{ct}^{\star},\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{vk}^{\star})\leftarrow\mathsf{SKECD}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk},r_{b})
and sends
\mathsf{skecd}.\mathpzc{ct}^{\star}
to
\mathpzc{R}
.

4.

\mathpzc{R}
initializes
\mathpzc{D}
with
1^{\lambda}
.
\mathpzc{D}
requests
q
keys for some polynomial
q
.

5.

For each
k\in[j]
,
\mathpzc{R}
computes
\mathpzc{dk}_{k}
as follows:

•

Sample a random value
\widetilde{r}\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{\ell_{\mathsf{m}}}
.

•

Compute
(\mathsf{skecd}.\widetilde{\mathpzc{ct}},\mathsf{skecd}.\widetilde{\mathsf{vk}})\leftarrow O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{Enc}}}(\widetilde{r})
.

•

Compute
\mathpzc{dk}_{k}
by executing Steps 2.(c)-2.(g) as in

\mathsf{Hyb}_{j}^{0}
, but using
\mathsf{skecd}.\widetilde{\mathpzc{ct}}
in place of

\mathsf{skecd}.\mathpzc{ct}
.

6.

\mathpzc{R}
computes
\mathpzc{dk}_{j+1}
by executing Steps 2.(c)-2.(g) as
in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{j}^{0}
, but using
\mathsf{skecd}.\mathpzc{ct}^{\star}
in place of

\mathsf{skecd}.\mathpzc{ct}
.

7.

For each
k\in[j+2,q]
,
\mathpzc{R}
computes
\mathpzc{dk}_{k}
as follows:

•

Compute
(\mathsf{skecd}.\widetilde{\mathpzc{ct}},\mathsf{skecd}.\widetilde{\mathsf{vk}})\leftarrow O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{Enc}}}(r_{1})
.

•

Compute
\mathpzc{dk}_{k}
by executing Steps 2.(c)-2.(g) as in

\mathsf{Hyb}_{j}^{0}
, but using
\mathsf{skecd}.\widetilde{\mathpzc{ct}}
in place of

\mathsf{skecd}.\mathpzc{ct}
.

8.

\mathpzc{R}
sends
\mathpzc{dk}_{1},\ldots,\mathpzc{dk}_{q}
to
\mathpzc{D}
and initializes

V_{k}=\bot
for every
k\in[q]
.

9.

If
k\neq j+1
,
\mathpzc{R}
simulates
the response of oracle
O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{Vrfy}}}(k,\widetilde{\mathpzc{dk}})
as follows:

•

Parse
\widetilde{\mathsf{vk}}=(x,\theta,S=\{s_{i,0}\oplus s_{i,1}\}_{i\in[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]\;:\;\theta[i]=1})
and
\widetilde{\mathpzc{dk}}=(\rho_{i})_{i\in[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]}
.

•

For every
i\in[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]
, measure
\rho_{i}
in the Hadamard
basis to get outcomes
c_{i},d_{i}
corresponding to the registers

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{i}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{i}}}}
respectively.

•

Compute
\mathsf{cert}[i]=c_{i}\oplus d_{i}\cdot(s_{i,0}\oplus s_{i,1})
for every
i\in[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]
.

•

If
x[i]=\mathsf{cert}[i]
holds
for every
i\in[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]:\theta[i]=1
, then update
V_{k}=\top

and send
\top
to
\mathpzc{D}
. Else, send
\bot
.

10.

If
k=j+1
,
\mathpzc{R}
simulates
the response of oracle
O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{Vrfy}}}(k,\widetilde{\mathpzc{dk}})
as
follows:

•

Compute
\mathsf{cert}=\mathsf{cert}[1]\|\ldots\|\mathsf{cert}[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]
,
where each
\mathsf{cert}[i]
is computed as in Step 9.

•

Send
\mathsf{cert}
to
\mathpzc{Ch}
. If
\mathpzc{Ch}
returns
\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk}
, send
\top
to
\mathpzc{D}
and update
V_{j+1}=\top
. Else if
\mathpzc{Ch}
returns
\bot
, send
\bot
to
\mathpzc{D}
.

11.

\mathpzc{D}
sends
(\mathsf{m}_{0}^{\star},\mathsf{m}_{1}^{\star})\in\{0,1\}^{\ell_{\mathsf{m}}}\times\{0,1\}^{\ell_{\mathsf{m}}}
to
\mathpzc{R}
. If
V_{i}=\bot
for any
i\in[q]
,
\mathpzc{R}

sends
0
to
\mathpzc{Ch}
.

\mathpzc{R}
computes
\mathsf{ct}^{\star}=(\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk},r_{1}\oplus\mathsf{m}^{\star}_{\mathsf{coin}})
,
where
\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk}
is obtained from
\mathpzc{Ch}
in Step 10.
\mathpzc{R}
sends
\mathsf{ct}^{*}
to
\mathpzc{D}
.

12.

\mathpzc{D}
outputs a guess
b^{\prime}
which
\mathpzc{R}
forwards to
\mathpzc{Ch}
.

\mathpzc{Ch}
outputs
b^{\prime}
as the final output of the experiment.

We will first argue that when
b=1
, the view of
\mathpzc{D}
is exactly
the same as its view in the hybrid
\mathsf{Hyb}_{j}^{\mathsf{coin}}
. Notice that the
reduction computes the first
j
decryption keys by querying the
encryption oracle on random plaintexts.
\mathsf{Hyb}_{j}
on the other hand,
directly computes them but there is no difference in the
output ciphertexts. A similar argument holds for the keys

\mathpzc{dk}_{j+2},\ldots,\mathpzc{dk}_{q}
, which contain encryptions of the same
random value
r_{1}
. Moreover, if
b=1
, the value encrypted as part
of the key
\mathpzc{dk}_{j+1}
is also
r_{1}
. This is the same as in

\mathsf{Hyb}_{j}^{\mathsf{coin}}
. As for the verification oracle queries, notice that
they are answered similarly by the reduction and
\mathsf{Hyb}_{j}^{\mathsf{coin}}
for
all but the
j+1
-th key. For the
j+1
-th key, the reduction works
differently in that it forwards the certificate
\mathsf{cert}
to the
verification oracle. However, the verification procedure of the
BB84-based SKE-CD scheme checks the validity of the value
\mathsf{cert}
in
the same way as the reduction, so there is no difference.

Finally, notice that when
b=0
, the encrypted value is random and
independent of
r_{1}
, similar to the hybrid
\mathsf{Hyb}_{j+1}^{\mathsf{coin}}
.
Consequently,
\mathpzc{R}
breaks the IND-CVA-CD security of
\mathsf{SKECD}
with
non-negligible probability, a contradiction.
∎

Notice now that the hybrid
\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}^{\mathsf{coin}}
is the same as the
experiment
\mathsf{Exp}_{\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{ot}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ind}\mbox{-}\mathsf{kla}}\allowbreak(1^{\lambda},\mathsf{coin})
. From
the previous lemma, we have that
\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}^{\mathsf{coin}}\approx\mathsf{Hyb}_{q}^{\mathsf{coin}}
. However, we have that
\mathsf{Hyb}_{q}^{0}\approx\mathsf{Hyb}_{q}^{1}

because
\mathsf{Hyb}_{q}^{0}
and
\mathsf{Hyb}_{q}^{1}
do not encrypt
r
at all as part
of the decryption keys, but they mask the plaintext with
r
.
Consequently, we have that
\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}^{0}\approx\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}^{1}
, which
completes the proof. ∎

5.3
Proof of Key-Testability

First, we will argue the correctness requirement. Recall that

\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{KG}
applies the following map to a BB84 state

\ket{x}_{\theta}
, where
(x,\theta)\in\{0,1\}^{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}}\times\{0,1\}^{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}}
, for every
i\in[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]
:

\displaystyle\ket{u_i}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{i}}}}}\otimes\ket{v_i}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{i}}}}}\rightarrow\ket{u_i}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{i}}}}}\otimes\ket{v_i\oplus s_{i,u_i}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{i}}}}}

(39)

where
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{i}}}}
denotes the register holding the
i
-th
qubit of
\ket{x}_{\theta}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{i}}}}
is a register initialized
to
\ket{0\ldots 0}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{i}}}}}
.

Consider applying the algorithm
\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{KeyTest}
in
superposition to the resulting state, i.e., performing the following
map, where
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{\mathsf{SKECD}.CT}}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{\mathsf{SKECD}.CT_{1}}}}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{\mathsf{SKECD}.CT_{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S}}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{1}}}}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}}}}}
, and

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{KT}}}
is initialized to
\ket{0}
:

\displaystyle\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT}}}}\otimes\ket{v}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S}}}}\otimes\ket{\beta}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{KT}}}}\rightarrow\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT}}}}\otimes\ket{v}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S}}}}\otimes\ket{\beta\oplus\SKECRSKL.
\KeyTest(\tk, u\|v)}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{KT}}}}

(40)

where
\mathsf{tk}=T=t_{1,0}\|t_{1,1}\|\cdots\|t_{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}},0}\|t_{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}},1}
. Recall that
\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{KeyTest}

outputs 1 if
and only if
\mathsf{Check}[t_{i,0},t_{i,1}](u_{i},v_{i})=1
for every
i\in[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]
, where
u_{i},v_{i}
denote the states of the registers

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{i}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{i}}}}
respectively.
Recall that
\mathsf{Check}[t_{i,0},t_{i,1}](u_{i},v_{i})
computes
f(v_{i})

and checks if it equals
t_{i,u_{i}}
. Since the construction chooses

t_{i,u_{i}}
such that
f(s_{i,u_{i}})=t_{i,u_{i}}
, this check
always passes. Consequently, measuring register
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{KT}}}
always
produces outcome
1
.

We will now argue that the security requirement holds by showing the
following reduction to the security of the OWF
f
. Let
\mathpzc{A}
be an
adversary that breaks the key-testability of
\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}
. Consider
a QPT reduction
\mathpzc{R}
that works as follows in the OWF security
experiment:

Execution of
\mathpzc{R}{A}
in

\mathsf{Expt}_{f,\mathpzc{R}}^{\mathsf{owf}}(1^{\lambda})
:

1.

The challenger chooses
s\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{\lambda}
and sends

y\coloneqq f(s)
to
\mathpzc{R}
.

2.

\mathpzc{R}
runs
\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
and initializes
\mathpzc{A}

with input
\mathsf{msk}
.

3.

\mathpzc{A}
requests
q
decryption keys for some polynomial
q
.

\mathpzc{R}
picks a random index
k^{\star}\in[q]
. For every
k\neq k^{\star}
,
\mathpzc{R}
generates
(\mathpzc{dk}_{k},\mathsf{vk}_{k},\mathsf{tk}_{k})
by computing the
function
f
as needed. For the index
k^{\star}
,
\mathpzc{R}
computes

(\mathpzc{dk}_{k^{\star}},\mathsf{vk}_{k^{\star}},\mathsf{tk}_{k^{\star}})
as follows (the
difference is colored in
red
):

(a)

Parse
\mathsf{msk}=(\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk},r)
.

(b)

Generate

(\mathsf{skecd}.\mathpzc{ct},\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{vk})\leftarrow\mathsf{SKECD}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk},r)
.

\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{vk}
is of the form

(x,\theta)\in\{0,1\}^{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}}\times\{0,1\}^{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}}
,

\mathsf{skecd}.\mathpzc{ct}
is of the form

\ket{\psi_1}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{1}}}}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\ket{\psi_{\ctlen}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}}}}}}
.

(c)

Choose an index
i^{\star}\in[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]
such
that
\theta[i^{\star}]=0
. For every
i\in[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]
such
that
i\neq i^{\star}
, generate
s_{i,b}\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{\lambda}
and compute

t_{i,b}\leftarrow f(s_{i,b})
for every
b\in\{0,1\}
. For
i=i^{\star}
,
set
t_{i^{\star},1-x[i^{\star}]}\coloneqq y
. Then, generate
s_{i^{\star},x[i^{\star}]}\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{\lambda}
and compute
t_{i^{\star},x[i^{\star}]}=f(s_{i^{\star},x[i^{\star}]})
.
Set
T\coloneqq t_{1,0}\|t_{1,1}\|\cdots\|t_{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}},0}\|t_{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}},1}
and
S=\{s_{i,0}\oplus s_{i,1}\}_{i\in[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]\;:\;\theta[i]=1}
.

(d)

Prepare a register
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{i}}}}
that is initialized to

\ket{0\cdots 0}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{i}}}}}
for every
i\in[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]
.

(e)

For every
i\in[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]
, apply the map

\displaystyle\ket{u_i}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{i}}}}}\otimes\ket{v_i}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{i}}}}}\rightarrow\ket{u_i}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{i}}}}}\otimes\ket{v_i\oplus s_{i,u_i}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{i}}}}}

(41)

to the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{i}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{i}}}}
and obtain the resulting state
\rho_{i}
.

(f)

Compute
\mathpzc{dk}_{k^{\star}}=(\rho_{i})_{i\in{[\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}]}}
,

\mathsf{vk}_{k^{\star}}=(x,\theta,S)
, and
\mathsf{tk}_{k^{\star}}=T
.

4.

\mathpzc{R}
sends
(\mathpzc{dk}_{i},\mathsf{vk}_{i},\mathsf{tk}_{i})
to
\mathpzc{A}
for every
i\in[q]
.

5.

\mathpzc{A}
sends
(k,\mathsf{dk},\mathsf{m})
to
\mathpzc{R}
. If
k\neq k^{\star}
,
\mathpzc{R}

aborts. Otherwise,
\mathpzc{R}
parses
\mathsf{dk}

as a string over the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT}}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{1}}}}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S}}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{1}}}}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}}}}}
and measures the register

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{i^{\star}}}}}
to obtain an outcome
s_{i^{\star}}
.
\mathpzc{R}

then sends
s_{i^{\star}}
to the challenger.

Notice that the view of
\mathpzc{A}
is the same as its view in the
key-testability experiment, as only the value
t_{i^{\star},1-x[i^{\star}]}
is generated differently by forwarding the value
y
,
but this value is distributed identically to the original value.
Note that in both cases,
\mathpzc{A}
receives no information about a
pre-image of
t_{i^{\star},1-x[i^{\star}]}
.
Now,
\mathpzc{R}
guesses the index
k
that
\mathpzc{A}
targets with probability

\frac{1}{q}
. By assumption, we have that
\mathsf{CDec}(\mathsf{dk},\mathsf{ct})\neq\mathsf{m}
where
\mathsf{ct}=\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{msk},\mathsf{m})
. The value
\mathsf{dk}
can be
parsed as a string over the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S}}}
. Let
\widetilde{\mathsf{dk}}
be the sub-string of
\mathsf{dk}
on the
register
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT}}}
. Recall that
\mathsf{CDec}
invokes the
algorithm
\mathsf{SKECD}.\mathsf{CDec}
on input
\widetilde{\mathsf{dk}}
. We will
now recall a property of
\mathsf{SKECD}.\mathsf{CDec}
that was specified in
Definition
3.12
:

Let
(\mathpzc{ct},\mathsf{vk}=(x,\theta))\leftarrow\mathsf{SKECD}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk},r)

where
\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk}\leftarrow\mathsf{SKECD}.\mathsf{KG}(1^{\lambda})
. Now, let
u
be any
arbitrary value such that
u[i]=x[i]
for all
i:\theta[i]=0
.
Then, the following holds:

\Pr[\mathsf{SKECD}.\mathsf{CDec}(\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk},u)=r\Big]\geq 1-{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda)

Consequently, if
\widetilde{\mathsf{dk}}
is such that
\widetilde{\mathsf{dk}}[i]=x[i]
for all
i:\theta[i]=0
, where
(x,\theta)
are
specified by
\mathsf{vk}_{k^{\star}}
, then
\mathsf{SKECD}.\mathsf{CDec}(\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk},\widetilde{\mathsf{dk}})
outputs the value
r
with high probability. Since

\mathsf{CDec}(\mathsf{dk},\mathsf{ct}=(\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk},r\oplus\mathsf{m}))
outputs
r\oplus\mathsf{m}\oplus\mathsf{SKECD}.\mathsf{CDec}(\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk},\widetilde{\mathsf{dk}})
, we have that

\mathsf{CDec}(\mathsf{dk},\mathsf{ct}=(\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk},r\oplus\mathsf{m}))=\mathsf{m}
. Therefore, it must
be the case that there exists some index
i
for which

\widetilde{\mathsf{dk}}[i]\neq x[i]
. With probability
\frac{1}{\ell_{\mathsf{ct}}}
,
this happens to be the guessed value
i^{\star}
. In this case,
\mathpzc{A}

must output
s_{i^{\star}}
on register
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{S_{i}}}}
such that

f(s_{i^{\star}})=t_{i^{\star},1-x[i^{\star}]}=y
. This concludes
the proof. ∎

Since we have proved OT-IND-KLA security (Section

5.2
) and Key-Testability (Section

5.3
), we can now state the following theorem:

6
PKE-CR-SKL from LWE

In this section, we show how to achieve PKE-CR-SKL from SKE-CR-SKL, standard ABE, and compute-and-compare obfuscation.

6.1
Construction

We construct a PKE-CR-SKL scheme

\mathsf{PKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}=\mathsf{PKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.(\mathsf{Setup},\mathpzc{KG},\allowbreak\mathsf{Enc},\mathpzc{Dec},\mathpzc{Vrfy})
with
message space
\mathcal{M}=\{0,1\}^{\ell}
using the following building blocks.

•

ABE scheme
\mathsf{ABE}=\mathsf{ABE}.(\mathsf{Setup},\mathsf{KG},\mathsf{Enc},\mathsf{Dec})
for the following relation
R
.

R(x,y)
:

Interpret
x
as a circuit. Then, output
0
(decryptable) if
\bot=x(y)
and otherwise
1
.

•

Compute-and-Compare Obfuscation
\mathsf{CC}.\mathsf{Obf}
with the simulator
\mathsf{CC}.\mathsf{Sim}
.

•

SKE-CR-SKL scheme with Key Testability

\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}=\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.(\mathsf{Setup},\allowbreak\mathpzc{KG},\mathsf{Enc},\mathpzc{Dec},\mathpzc{Vrfy},\mathsf{KeyTest})
. It also has the classical decryption algorithm
\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{CDec}
.

The construction is as follows.

\mathsf{PKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
:

•

Generate
(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{msk})\leftarrow\mathsf{ABE}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
.

•

Generate
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{msk}\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
.

•

Output
\mathsf{ek}\coloneqq\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk}
and
\mathsf{msk}\coloneqq(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{msk},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{msk})
.

\mathsf{PKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{KG}(\mathsf{msk})
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{msk}=(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{msk},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{msk})
.

•

Generate
(\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{dk},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{tk})\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{KG}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{msk})
. We denote the register holding
\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{dk}
as
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK}}}
.

•

Prepare a register
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}
that is initialized to
\ket{0\cdots 0}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}}
.

•

Choose explicit randomness
\mathsf{k}\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{\lambda}
.

•

Apply the map

\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK}}}}\ket{v}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}}\rightarrow\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK}}}}\ket{v\oplus\ABE.\KG(\abe.\msk,u,
\key)}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}}
to the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}
, and obtain
\mathpzc{dk}
over the registers

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}
.

•

Output
\mathpzc{dk}
and

\mathsf{vk}\coloneqq(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{msk},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{tk},\mathsf{k})
.

\mathsf{PKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{ek},\mathsf{m})
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{ek}=\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk}
.

•

Generate
\widetilde{C}\leftarrow\mathsf{CC}.\mathsf{Sim}(1^{\lambda},\mathsf{pp}_{D},1)
, where
\mathsf{pp}_{D}
consists of circuit parameters of
D
defined in the security proof.

•

Generate
\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{ct}\leftarrow\mathsf{ABE}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk},\widetilde{C},\mathsf{m})
.

•

Output
\mathsf{ct}\coloneqq\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{ct}
.

\mathsf{PKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{Dec}(\mathpzc{dk},\mathsf{ct})
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{ct}=\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{ct}
. We denote the register holding
\mathpzc{dk}
as
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK}}}\otimes{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}
.

•

Prepare a register
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{MSG}}}
that is initialized to
\ket{0\cdots 0}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{MSG}}}}

•

Apply the map
\ket{v}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}}\ket{w}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{MSG}}}}\rightarrow\ket{v}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}}\ket{w\oplus\ABE.\Dec(v,\abe.\ct)}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{MSG}}}}
to the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{MSG}}}
.

•

Measure the register
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{MSG}}}
in the computational basis and output the result
\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
.

\mathsf{PKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\mathpzc{dk}^{\prime})
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{vk}=(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{msk},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{tk},\mathsf{k})
. We denote the register holding
\mathpzc{dk}^{\prime}
as
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK}}}\otimes{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}
.

•

Prepare a register
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.KT}}}
that is initialized to
\ket{0}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.KT}}}}
.

•

Apply the map
\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK}}}}\ket{\beta}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.KT}}}}\rightarrow\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK}}}}\ket{\beta\oplus\SKECRSKL.\KeyTest(\ske.\tk,u)}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.KT}}}}
to the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.KT}}}
.

•

Measure
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.KT}}}
in the computational basis and output
\bot
if the result is
0
. Otherwise, go to the next step.

•

Apply the map

\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK}}}}\ket{v}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}}\rightarrow\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK}}}}\ket{v\oplus\ABE.\KG(\abe.\msk,u,
\key)}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}}
to the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}
.

•

Trace out the register
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}
and obtain
\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{dk}^{\prime}
over
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK}}}
.

•

Output
\top
if
\top=\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{Vrfy}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{dk}^{\prime})

and
\bot
otherwise.

Decryption correctness.

The key

\mathpzc{dk}
output by
\mathsf{PKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{KG}
is of the form
\sum_{u}\alpha_{u}\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK}}}}\ket{\abe.\sk_u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}}
, where

\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{sk}_{u}\leftarrow\mathsf{ABE}.\mathsf{KG}(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{msk},u,\mathsf{k})
.
Let
\mathsf{ct}\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{ek},\mathsf{m})
.
On applying
\ket{v}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}}\ket{w}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{MSG}}}}\rightarrow\ket{v}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}}\ket{w\oplus\ABE.\Dec(v,\abe.\ct)}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{MSG}}}}
to
\sum_{u}\alpha_{u}\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK}}}}\ket{\abe.\sk_u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}}\otimes\ket{0\cdots 0}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{MSG}}}}
, with overwhelming probability, the result is negligibly close to

\displaystyle\sum_{u}\alpha_{u}\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK}}}}\ket{\abe.\sk_u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}}\otimes\ket{\msg}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{MSG}}}}

(42)

since
\widetilde{C}(u)=\bot
and thus
R(\widetilde{C},u)=0
for
\widetilde{C}\leftarrow\mathsf{CC}.\mathsf{Sim}(1^{\lambda},\mathsf{pp}_{D},1)
and almost every string
u
.
Therefore, we see that
\mathsf{PKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}
satisfies decryption correctness.

Verification correctness.

Let
\mathpzc{dk}\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{KG}(\mathsf{msk})
.
It is clear that the state
\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{dk}^{\prime}
obtained when computing
\mathsf{PKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\mathpzc{dk})
is the same as
\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{dk}
generated when generating
\mathpzc{dk}
.
Therefore, the verification correctness of
\mathsf{PKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}
follows from that of
\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}
.

6.2
Proof of IND-KLA Security

Let
\mathpzc{A}
be an adversary for the IND-KLA security of
\mathsf{PKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}
.
We consider the following sequence of experiments.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}^{\mathsf{coin}}
:

This is
\mathsf{Exp}_{\mathsf{PKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{ind}\mbox{-}\mathsf{kla}}(1^{\lambda},\mathsf{coin})
.

1.

The challenger
\mathpzc{Ch}
generates
(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{msk})\leftarrow\mathsf{ABE}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
and
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{msk}\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
, and sends
\mathsf{ek}\coloneqq\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk}
to
\mathpzc{A}
.

2.

\mathpzc{A}
requests
q
decryption keys for some polynomial
q
.

\mathpzc{Ch}
generates
\mathpzc{dk}_{i}
as follows for every
i\in[q]
:

•

Generate
(\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{dk}_{i},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{vk}_{i},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{tk}_{i})\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{KG}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{msk})
. We denote the register holding
\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{dk}_{i}
as
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK_{i}}}}
.

•

Prepare a register
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{i}}}}
that is initialized to
\ket{0\cdots 0}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{i}}}}}
.

•

Choose explicit randomness
\mathsf{k}_{i}\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{\lambda}
.

•

Apply the map

\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK_{i}}}}}\ket{v}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{i}}}}}\rightarrow\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK_{i}}}}}\ket{v\oplus\ABE.\KG(\abe.\msk,u,
\key_i)}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{i}}}}}
to the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK_{i}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{i}}}}
, and obtain
\mathpzc{dk}_{i}
over the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK_{i}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{i}}}}
.

\mathpzc{Ch}
sends
\mathpzc{dk}_{1},\ldots,\mathpzc{dk}_{q}
to
\mathpzc{A}
.

3.

\mathpzc{A}
can get access to the following (stateful) verification
oracle
O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{Vrfy}}}
where
V_{i}
is initialized to be
\bot
:

O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{Vrfy}}}(i,\widetilde{\mathpzc{dk}})
:

It computes
d
as follows.

(a)

Let the register holding
\widetilde{\mathpzc{dk}}
be
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK_{i}}}}\otimes{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{i}}}}
.

(b)

Prepare a register
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.KT_{i}}}}
that is initialized to
\ket{0}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.KT_{i}}}}}
.

(c)

Apply the map
\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK_{i}}}}}\ket{\beta}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.KT_{i}}}}}\rightarrow\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK_{i}}}}}\ket{\beta\oplus\SKECRSKL.\KeyTest(\ske.\tk_i,u)}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.KT_{i}}}}}
to the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK_{i}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.KT_{i}}}}
.

(d)

Measure
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.KT_{i}}}}
in the computational basis and
set
d\coloneqq\bot
if the result is
0
. Otherwise, go to the next step.

(e)

Apply the map

\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK_{i}}}}}\ket{v}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{i}}}}}\rightarrow\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK_{i}}}}}\ket{v\oplus\ABE.\KG(\abe.\msk,u,\key_i)}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{i}}}}}
to the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK_{i}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{i}}}}
.

(f)

Trace out the register
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{i}}}}
and obtain
\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{dk}^{\prime}
over
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK_{i}}}}
.

(g)

Set
d\coloneqq\top
if

\top=\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{Vrfy}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{vk}_{i},\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{dk}^{\prime})
and set
d\coloneqq\bot
otherwise.
It returns
d
to
\mathpzc{A}
. Finally, if
V_{i}=\bot
and
d=\top
, it updates
V_{i}\coloneqq\top
.

4.

\mathpzc{A}
sends
(\mathsf{m}_{0}^{*},\mathsf{m}_{1}^{*})\in\mathcal{M}^{2}
to

\mathpzc{Ch}
. If
V_{i}=\bot
for some
i\in[q]
,

\mathpzc{Ch}
outputs
0
as the final output of this
experiment. Otherwise,
\mathpzc{Ch}
generates

\widetilde{C}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{CC}.\mathsf{Sim}(1^{\lambda},\mathsf{pp}_{D},1)
and
\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{ABE}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk},\widetilde{C}^{*},\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{coin}}^{*})
, and sends
\mathsf{ct}^{*}\coloneqq\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}
to
\mathpzc{A}
.

5.

\mathpzc{A}
outputs
\mathsf{coin}^{\prime}
.
\mathpzc{Ch}
outputs
\mathsf{coin}^{\prime}
as the final output of the experiment.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}^{\mathsf{coin}}
:

This is the same as
\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}^{\mathsf{coin}}
except
that
\widetilde{C}^{*}
is generated as

\widetilde{C}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{CC}.\mathsf{Obf}(1^{\lambda},D[\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}],\mathsf{lock},0)
, where

\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{msk},0^{\lambda})
,

\mathsf{lock}\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{\lambda}
, and
D[\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}](x)
is a circuit that has

\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}
hardwired and outputs
\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{CDec}(x,\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{ct}^{*})
.

We pick
\mathsf{lock}
as a uniformly random string that is completely independent of other variables such as
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}
.
Thus, from the security of
\mathsf{CC}.\mathsf{Obf}
, we have
\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}^{\mathsf{coin}}\approx\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}^{\mathsf{coin}}
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}^{\mathsf{coin}}
:

This is the same as
\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}^{\mathsf{coin}}
except
that
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}
hardwired into the obfuscated circuit
\widetilde{C}^{*}
is
generated as
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{msk},\mathsf{lock})
.

From the OT-IND-KLA security of
\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}
, we can show that

\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}^{\mathsf{coin}}\approx\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}^{\mathsf{coin}}
. Suppose that
\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}^{\mathsf{coin}}\not\approx\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}^{\mathsf{coin}}
and
\mathpzc{D}
is a corresponding
distinguisher. We consider the following reduction

\mathpzc{R}
:

Execution of
\mathpzc{R}^{\mathpzc{D}}
in

\mathsf{Exp}_{\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL},\mathpzc{R}}^{\mathsf{ot}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ind}\mbox{-}\mathsf{kla}}(1^{\lambda},b)
:

1.

\mathpzc{Ch}
runs
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{msk}\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
and initializes
\mathpzc{R}

with the security parameter
1^{\lambda}
.

2.

\mathpzc{R}
generates
(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{msk})\leftarrow\mathsf{ABE}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
and sends
\mathsf{ek}\coloneqq\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk}
to
\mathpzc{D}
.

3.

\mathpzc{D}
requests
q
decryption keys for some polynomial
q
.

\mathpzc{R}
requests
q
decryption keys.
\mathpzc{Ch}
generates
(\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{dk}_{i},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{vk}_{i},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{tk}_{i})\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{KG}(\mathsf{msk})
for every
i\in[q]

and sends
(\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{dk}_{i},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{tk}_{i})_{i\in[q]}
to
\mathpzc{R}
.

4.

\mathpzc{R}
computes
\mathpzc{dk}_{1},\ldots,\mathpzc{dk}_{q}
as in Step
2.
of

\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}^{\mathsf{coin}}
, except that the received values

(\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{dk}_{i})_{i\in[q]}
are used instead of the original ones.

5.

\mathpzc{R}
simulates the access to
O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{Vrfy}}}(i,\widetilde{\mathpzc{dk}})
for
\mathpzc{D}
as follows:

O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{Vrfy}}}(i,\widetilde{\mathpzc{dk}}):

(a)

Perform Step
3.(\textrm{a})
-
3.(\textrm{f})
of
\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}^{\mathsf{coin}}
to obtain

\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{dk}^{\prime}
, but using the received value
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{tk}_{i}

instead of the original one.

(b)

Set
d\coloneqq\top
if
\top=\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{Vrfy}}}(i,\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{dk}^{\prime})
and set
d\coloneqq\bot

otherwise.
It returns
d
to
\mathpzc{D}
. Finally, if
V_{i}=\bot
and
d=\top
, it
updates
V_{i}=\top
.

6.

\mathpzc{R}
sends
(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{m}_{0}^{*},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{m}_{1}^{*})\coloneqq(0^{\lambda},\mathsf{lock})

to
\mathpzc{Ch}
and receives
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{msk},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{m}^{*}_{b})
.

7.

\mathpzc{D}
sends
(\mathsf{m}_{0}^{*},\mathsf{m}_{1}^{*})\in\mathcal{M}^{2}
to
\mathpzc{R}
. If
V_{i}=\bot
for some
i\in[q]
,
\mathpzc{R}
outputs
0
. Otherwise,
\mathpzc{R}

generates
\widetilde{C}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{CC}.\mathsf{Obf}(1^{\lambda},D[\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}],\mathsf{lock},0)

and
\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{ABE}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk},\widetilde{C}^{*},\mathsf{m}^{*}_{\mathsf{coin}})
and
sends
\mathsf{ct}^{*}\coloneqq\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}
to
\mathpzc{D}
.

8.

\mathpzc{D}
outputs a bit
b^{\prime}
.
\mathpzc{R}
outputs
b^{\prime}
and
\mathpzc{Ch}

outputs
b^{\prime}
as the final output of the experiment.

It is easy to see that the view of
\mathpzc{D}
is the same as that in

\mathsf{Hyb}^{\mathsf{coin}}_{2}
when
\mathsf{lock}
is encrypted in
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}
and that of

\mathsf{Hyb}^{\mathsf{coin}}_{1}
when
0^{\lambda}
is encrypted. Moreover, for
\mathpzc{D}
to
distinguish between the two hybrids, it must be the case that
V_{i}=\top
for all
i\in[q]
, which directly implies that the
q

analogous values checked by
\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{Vrfy}}}
must also be

\top
. Consequently,
\mathpzc{R}
breaks the OT-IND-KLA security of

\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}
. Therefore, it must be that
\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}^{\mathsf{coin}}\approx\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}^{\mathsf{coin}}
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}^{\mathsf{coin}}
:

This is the same as
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}^{\mathsf{coin}}
except
that
\mathpzc{dk}_{i}
is generated as follows for every
i\in[q]
. (The
difference is colored in red.)

•

Generate
(\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{dk}_{i},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{vk}_{i},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{tk}_{i})\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{KG}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{msk})
. We denote the register holding
\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{dk}_{i}
as
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK_{i}}}}
.

•

Prepare a register
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.R_{i}}}}
that is initialized to
\ket{0}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.R_{i}}}}}
.

•

Apply the map
\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK_{i}}}}}\ket{\beta}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.R_{i}}}}}\rightarrow\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK_{i}}}}}\ket{\beta\oplus R(\tlC^*,u)}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.R_{i}}}}}

to the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK_{i}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.R_{i}}}}
. (Note that we
can generate
\widetilde{C}^{*}
at the beginning of the game.)

•

Measure
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.R_{i}}}}
in the computational basis and set
\mathpzc{dk}_{i}\coloneqq\bot
if the result is
0
. Otherwise, go to the next step.

•

Prepare a register
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{i}}}}
that is initialized to
\ket{0\cdots 0}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{i}}}}}
.

•

Sample explicit randomness
\mathsf{k}_{i}\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{\lambda}
.

•

Apply the map

\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK_{i}}}}}\ket{v}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{i}}}}}\rightarrow\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK_{i}}}}}\ket{v\oplus\ABE.\KG(\abe.\msk,u,\key_i)}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{i}}}}}
to the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK_{i}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{i}}}}
, and obtain
\mathpzc{dk}_{i}
over the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK_{i}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{i}}}}
.

From the decryption correctness of
\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}
, the added procedure that checks
R(\widetilde{C}^{*},u)
in superposition does not affect the final state
\mathpzc{dk}_{i}
with overwhelming probability since
R(\widetilde{C}^{*},u)=1
in this hybrid for any
u
that appears in
\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{dk}_{i}
when describing it in the computational basis.
Therefore, we have
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}^{\mathsf{coin}}\approx\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}^{\mathsf{coin}}
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{4}^{\mathsf{coin}}
:

This is the same as
\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}^{\mathsf{coin}}
except
that the oracle
O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{Vrfy}}}
behaves as follows. (The
difference is colored in red.)

O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{Vrfy}}}(i,\widetilde{\mathpzc{dk}})

It computes
d
as follows.

(a)

Let the register holding

be
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK_{i}}}}\otimes{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{i}}}}
.

(b)

Prepare a register

.

(c)

Apply the map

to the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK_{i}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.KT_{i}}}}
.

Measure

(e)

.

Apply the map
