to the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK_{i}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.R_{i}}}}
.

(g)

Measure
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.R_{i}}}}
in the computational basis and set
d\coloneqq\bot
if the result is
0
. Otherwise, go to the next step.

(h)

Apply the map

\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK_{i}}}}}\ket{v}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{i}}}}}\rightarrow\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK_{i}}}}}\ket{v\oplus\ABE.\KG(\abe.\msk,u,\key_i)}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{i}}}}}
to the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK_{i}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{i}}}}
.

(i)

Trace out the register
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{i}}}}
and obtain
\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{dk}^{\prime}
over
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK_{i}}}}
.

(j)

Set
d\coloneqq\top
if
\top=\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{Vrfy}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{vk}_{i},\mathsf{ske}.\mathpzc{dk}^{\prime})
and set
d\coloneqq\bot
otherwise.
Return
d
to
\mathpzc{A}
. Finally, if
V_{i}=\bot
and
d=\top
, update
V_{i}\coloneqq\top
.

Suppose there exists a QPT distinguisher
\mathpzc{D}
that has
non-negligible advantage in distinguishing
\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}^{\mathsf{coin}}
and

\mathsf{Hyb}_{4}^{\mathsf{coin}}
. Let
\mathpzc{D}
make
q={\mathrm{poly}}(\lambda)
many queries to
the oracle
O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{Vrfy}}}(\cdot,\cdot)
. We will now consider the
following QPT algorithm
\mathpzc{A}_{\mathsf{o2h}}
with access to an oracle

O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
and an oracle
\mathcal{O}
that runs
\mathpzc{D}
as follows:

\underline{\mathpzc{A}_{\mathsf{o2h}}^{O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}},\mathcal{O}}(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{msk})}:

1.

\mathpzc{A}_{\mathsf{o2h}}
initializes
\mathpzc{D}
with input
\mathsf{ek}=\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk}
.

2.

When
\mathpzc{D}
requests
q
decryption keys,
\mathpzc{A}_{\mathsf{o2h}}
queries

O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
on input
q
and receives
(\{\mathpzc{dk}_{i}\}_{i\in[q]},\{\mathsf{vk}_{i}\}_{i\in[q]})
. It forwards
\{\mathpzc{dk}_{i}\}_{i\in[q]}
to
\mathpzc{D}
.

3.

\mathpzc{A}_{\mathsf{o2h}}
simulates the access of
O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{Vrfy}}}
for
\mathpzc{D}
as
follows:

\underline{O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{Vrfy}}}(y,\widetilde{\mathpzc{dk}})}:

(a)

Execute Steps (a)-(e) of
O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{Vrfy}}}
as in

\mathsf{Hyb}_{4}^{\mathsf{coin}}
.

(b)

Apply the map

\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK_{y}}}}}\ket{\beta}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.R_{y}}}}}\rightarrow\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK_{y}}}}}\ket{\beta\oplus\calO(u)}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.R_{y}}}}}
to the registers

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.DK_{y}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.R_{y}}}}
.

(c)

Execute Steps (g)-(j) of
O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{Vrfy}}}
as in

\mathsf{Hyb}_{4}^{\mathsf{coin}}
.

4.

\mathpzc{D}
sends
(\mathsf{m}_{0}^{*},\mathsf{m}_{1}^{*})\in\mathcal{M}^{2}
to
\mathpzc{A}_{\mathsf{o2h}}
. If
V_{i}=\bot
for some
i\in[q]
,
\mathpzc{A}_{\mathsf{o2h}}
outputs
0
. Otherwise,

\mathpzc{A}_{\mathsf{o2h}}
generates
\widetilde{C}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{CC}.\mathsf{Obf}(1^{\lambda},D[\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}],\mathsf{lock},0)
, where
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{msk},\mathsf{lock})
.
It then generates
\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{ABE}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk},\widetilde{C}^{*},\mathsf{m}^{*}_{\mathsf{coin}})
and sends
\mathsf{ct}^{*}\coloneqq\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}
to
\mathpzc{D}
.

5.

\mathpzc{D}
outputs a guess
b^{\prime}
.
\mathpzc{A}_{\mathsf{o2h}}
outputs
b^{\prime}
.

Let
H
be an oracle that for every input
u
, outputs
1
.
Consider now the extractor
\mathpzc{B}_{\mathsf{o2h}}^{O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}},H}

as specified by the O2H Lemma (Lemma
3.2
). We will now
construct a reduction
\mathpzc{R}
that runs
\mathpzc{B}_{\mathsf{o2h}}
by simulating the oracles

O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
and
H
for
\mathpzc{B}_{\mathsf{o2h}}
, and breaks the
key-testability of the
\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}
scheme.

Execution of
\mathpzc{R}
in

\mathsf{Exp}_{\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL},\mathpzc{R}}^{\mathsf{key}\mbox{-}\mathsf{test}}(1^{\lambda})
:

1.

The challenger
\mathpzc{Ch}
runs
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{msk}\leftarrow\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
and
initializes
\mathpzc{R}
with input
\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{msk}
.

2.

\mathpzc{R}
samples
(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{msk})\leftarrow\mathsf{ABE}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
and initializes
\mathpzc{B}_{\mathsf{o2h}}
with the input

(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{msk})
.

3.

When
\mathpzc{B}_{\mathsf{o2h}}
queries input
q
to
O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
,
\mathpzc{R}

generates the decryption-keys
\mathpzc{dk}_{1},\ldots,\mathpzc{dk}_{q}
in the same way
as in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}^{\mathsf{coin}}
and sends them to
\mathpzc{B}_{\mathsf{o2h}}
.

4.

When
\mathpzc{B}_{\mathsf{o2h}}
queries an input
u
to
H
,
\mathpzc{R}
responds with
1
.

5.

\mathpzc{B}_{\mathsf{o2h}}
outputs measured index
y
and measurement outcome
\mathsf{dk}
.
\mathpzc{R}
sends
(y,\mathsf{dk},\mathsf{lock})

to
\mathpzc{Ch}
.

We will now claim that with non-negligible probability,
\mathpzc{R}
obtains
values
\mathsf{dk}
and
y
such that
R(\widetilde{C}^{*},\mathsf{dk})=0
. By the definition of

R
and
\widetilde{C}^{*}
and the decryption correctness of
\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}
, this
will imply that
\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{CDec}(\mathsf{dk},\allowbreak\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{ct}^{\star})\neq\mathsf{lock}
. Moreover,
\mathsf{KeyTest}(\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{tk}_{y},\mathsf{dk})
also holds.
Consequently, this will imply
\mathpzc{R}
breaks the key-testability of

\mathsf{SKE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}
. To prove this, we will rely on the One-Way to Hiding
(O2H) Lemma (Lemma
3.2
). Consider an oracle
G
which takes
as input
u
and outputs
R(\widetilde{C}^{*},u)
and an oracle
H
which takes
as input
u
and outputs
1
. Notice that if the oracle
\mathcal{O}=G
,
then the view of
\mathpzc{D}
as run by
\mathpzc{A}_{\mathsf{o2h}}
is the same as in

\mathsf{Hyb}_{4}^{\mathsf{coin}}
, while if
\mathcal{O}=H
, the view of
\mathpzc{D}
is the same as
in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}^{\mathsf{coin}}
. By the O2H Lemma, we have the following, where
z=(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{msk})
.

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\Pr[\qA_\oh^{\Oracle{\qKG},
H}(z)=1]-\Pr[\qA_\oh^{\Oracle{\qKG}, G}(z)=1]}\leq 2q\cdot\sqrt{\Pr[\mathpzc{B}_{\mathsf{o2h}}^{O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}},H}(z)\in S]}\kern 5.0pt.

(43)

where
S
is a set where
the oracles
H
and
G
differ, which happens only for inputs
u
s.t.

R(\widetilde{C}^{*},u)=0
. Since
\mathpzc{R}
obtains
\mathsf{dk}
and
y
as the
output of
\mathpzc{B}_{\mathsf{o2h}}^{O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}},H}(z)
, the argument
follows that
\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}^{\mathsf{coin}}\approx\mathsf{Hyb}_{4}^{\mathsf{coin}}
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{5}^{\mathsf{coin}}
:

This is the same as
\mathsf{Hyb}_{4}^{\mathsf{coin}}
except that
\mathsf{ct}^{*}\coloneqq\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}
is generated as
\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{ABE}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk},\widetilde{C}^{*},0^{\ell_{\mathsf{m}}})
.

The view of
\mathpzc{A}
in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{4}^{\mathsf{coin}}
and
\mathsf{Hyb}_{5}^{\mathsf{coin}}
can be
simulated with
\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk}
and the access to the quantum key
generation oracle
O_{\mathtt{qkg}}
. This is because before
\mathsf{ABE}.\mathsf{KG}

is required to be applied in both the generation of
\{\mathpzc{dk}_{i}\}_{i\in[q]}
and to compute the responses of
O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{Vrfy}}}
, the
relation check
R(\widetilde{C}^{*},u)
is already applied in
superposition. Thus, we have
\mathsf{Hyb}_{4}^{\mathsf{coin}}\approx\mathsf{Hyb}_{5}^{\mathsf{coin}}
.

Lastly,
\mathsf{Hyb}_{5}^{0}
and
\mathsf{Hyb}_{5}^{1}
are exactly the same experiment and thus we have
\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hybi{5}^0=1]-\Pr[\hybi{5}^1=1]}={\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda)
.
Then, from the above arguments, we obtain

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\Pr[\expb{\PKECRSKL,\qA}{ind}{kla}(1^\secp,0)=1]-\Pr[\expb{\PKECRSKL,\qA}{ind}{kla}(1^\secp,1)=1]}

(44)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hybi{0}^0=1]-\Pr[\hybi{0}^1=1]}\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

(45)

This completes the proof. ∎

Given the fact that SKE-CR-SKL with Key-Testability (implied by
BB84-based SKE-CD and OWFs), Compute-and-Compare Obfuscation, and
Ciphertext-Policy ABE for General Circuits are all implied by the
LWE assumption, we state the following theorem:

Theorem 6.1.

There exists a PKE-CR-SKL scheme satisfying IND-KLA security,
assuming the polynomial hardness of the LWE assumption.

7
ABE-CR-SKL from LWE

In this section, we show how to achieve ABE-CR-SKL from the LWE assumption.
To this end, we also introduce SKFE-CR-SKL with classical decryption and key-testability.
First, we recall the standard SKFE.

Definition 7.1 (Secret-Key Functional Encryption).

An SKFE scheme
\mathsf{SKFE}
for the functionality
F:\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}
is a tuple of four PPT algorithms
(\mathsf{Setup},\mathsf{KG},\mathsf{Enc},\mathsf{Dec})
.

\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})\rightarrow\mathsf{msk}
:

The setup algorithm takes a security parameter
1^{\lambda}
, and outputs a master secret key
\mathsf{msk}
.

\mathsf{KG}(\mathsf{msk},y)\rightarrow\mathsf{sk}_{y}
:

The key generation algorithm takes a master secret key
\mathsf{msk}
and a function
y\in\mathcal{Y}
, and outputs a functional decryption key
\mathsf{sk}_{y}
.

\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{msk},x)\rightarrow\mathsf{ct}
:

The encryption algorithm takes a master secret key
\mathsf{msk}
and a plaintext
x\in\mathcal{X}
, and outputs a ciphertext
\mathsf{ct}
.

\mathsf{Dec}(\mathsf{sk}_{y},\mathsf{ct})\rightarrow z
:

The decryption algorithm takes a
functional decryption key
\mathsf{sk}_{y}
and a ciphertext
\mathsf{ct}
, and
outputs
z\in\{\bot\}\cup\mathcal{Z}
.

Correctness:

We require that for every
x\in\mathcal{X}
and
y\in\mathcal{Y}
, we have that

\Pr\left[\mathsf{Dec}(\mathsf{sk}_{y},\mathsf{ct})=F(x,y)\ :\begin{array}[]{rl}&\mathsf{msk}\leftarrow\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})\\
&\mathsf{sk}_{y}\leftarrow\mathsf{KG}(\mathsf{msk},y)\\
&\mathsf{ct}\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{msk},x)\end{array}\right]\geq 1-{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

(46)

Definition 7.2 (Selective Single-Ciphertext Security).

We formalize the experiment

\mathsf{Exp}_{\mathsf{SKFE},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{adp}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ind}}(1^{\lambda},\mathsf{coin})
between an adversary

\mathpzc{A}
and a challenger for an SKFE scheme for the functionality
F:\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}
as follows:

1.

Initialized with
1^{\lambda}
,
\mathpzc{A}
outputs
(x_{0}^{*},x_{1}^{*})
. The challenger
\mathsf{Ch}
runs
\mathsf{msk}\leftarrow\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
and sends
\mathsf{ct}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{msk},x_{\mathsf{coin}}^{*})
to
\mathpzc{A}
.

2.

\mathpzc{A}
can get access to the following oracle.

O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}(y)
:

Given
y
, if
F(x_{0}^{*},y)\neq F(x_{1}^{*},y)
, returns
\bot
. Otherwise, it returns
\mathsf{sk}_{y}\leftarrow\mathsf{KG}(\mathsf{msk},y)
.

3.

\mathpzc{A}
outputs a guess
\mathsf{coin}^{\prime}
for
\mathsf{coin}
.

\mathpzc{Ch}
outputs
\mathsf{coin}^{\prime}
as the final output of the experiment.

We say that
\mathsf{SKFE}
satisfies selective single-ciphertext security if, for any QPT
\mathpzc{A}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathsf{Adv}_{\mathsf{SKFE},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{sel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{1ct}}(1^{\lambda})\coloneqq\absolutevalue{\Pr[\expb{\SKFE,\qA}{sel}{1ct} (1^\secp,0) \ra 1] - \Pr[\expb{\SKFE,\qA}{sel}{1ct} (1^\secp,1) \ra 1] }\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

(47)

Theorem 7.3 (
[
GVW12
]
).

Assuming the existence of OWFs, there exists selective single-ciphertext secure SKFE.

7.1
Definitions of SKFE-CR-SKL

We consider the classical decryption property and key-testability for
SKFE-CR-SKL as in the case of SKE-CR-SKL.

Definition 7.4 (SKFE-CR-SKL).

An SKFE-CR-SKL scheme
\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}
for the functionality
F:\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}
is a tuple
of five algorithms
(\mathsf{Setup},\mathpzc{KG},\mathsf{Enc},\allowbreak\mathpzc{Dec},\mathpzc{Vrfy})
.

\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})\rightarrow\mathsf{msk}
:

The setup algorithm takes a security parameter
1^{\lambda}
and a master secret key
\mathsf{msk}
.

\mathpzc{KG}(\mathsf{msk},y)\rightarrow(\mathpzc{sk}_{y},\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{tk})
:

The key generation algorithm
takes a master secret key
\mathsf{msk}
and a string
y\in\mathcal{Y}
, and
outputs a functional secret key
\mathpzc{sk}_{y}
, a certificate verification
key
\mathsf{vk}
, and a testing key
\mathsf{tk}
.

\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{msk},x)\rightarrow\mathsf{ct}
:

The encryption algorithm takes a master
secret key
\mathsf{msk}
and a string
x\in\mathcal{X}
, and outputs a ciphertext
\mathsf{ct}
.

\mathpzc{Dec}(\mathpzc{sk}_{y},\mathsf{ct})\rightarrow z
:

The decryption algorithm takes a
functional secret key
\mathpzc{sk}_{y}
and a ciphertext
\mathsf{ct}
, and outputs a
value
z\in\mathcal{Z}\cup\{\bot\}
.

\mathpzc{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\widetilde{\mathpzc{sk}}_{y})\rightarrow\top/\bot
:

The verification algorithm takes a verification key
\mathsf{vk}
and a (possibly malformed) functional secret key
\widetilde{\mathpzc{sk}}_{y}
, and outputs
\top
or
\bot
.

Decryption correctness:

For all
x\in\mathcal{X}
and

y\in\mathcal{Y}
, we have

\displaystyle\Pr\left[\mathpzc{Dec}(\mathpzc{sk}_{y},\mathsf{ct})\allowbreak=F(x,y)\ :\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathsf{msk}\leftarrow\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})\\
(\mathpzc{sk}_{y},\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{tk})\leftarrow\mathpzc{KG}(\mathsf{msk},y)\\
\mathsf{ct}\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{msk},x)\end{array}\right]\geq 1-{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

Verification correctness:

We have

\displaystyle\Pr\left[\mathpzc{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\mathpzc{sk}_{y})=\top\ :\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathsf{msk}\leftarrow\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})\\
(\mathpzc{sk}_{y},\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{tk})\leftarrow\mathpzc{KG}(\mathsf{msk},y)\end{array}\right]\geq 1-{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

Definition 7.5 (Classical Decryption Property).

We say that
\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}=(\mathsf{Setup},\mathpzc{KG},\mathsf{Enc},\mathpzc{Dec},\mathpzc{Vrfy})
has
the classical decryption property if there exists a deterministic
polynomial time algorithm
\mathsf{CDec}
such that given
\mathpzc{sk}_{y}
in the
register
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SK}}}
and ciphertext
\mathsf{ct}
,
\mathpzc{Dec}
applies the map

\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SK}}}}\ket{v}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{OUT}}}}\rightarrow\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SK}}}}\ket{v\oplus\CDec(u,\ct)}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{OUT}}}}

and outputs the measurement result of the register
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{OUT}}}
in
the computational basis, where
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{OUT}}}
is initialized to

\ket{0\cdots 0}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{OUT}}}}
.

Definition 7.6 (Key-Testability).

We say that an SKFE-CR-SKL scheme
\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}
with the classical
decryption property satisfies key testability, if there exists a
classical deterministic algorithm
\mathsf{KeyTest}
that satisfies the
following conditions:

•

Syntax:

\mathsf{KeyTest}
takes as input a testing key
\mathsf{tk}

and a classical string
\mathsf{sk}
as input. It outputs
0
or
1
.

•

Correctness:
Let
\mathsf{msk}\leftarrow\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
and

(\mathpzc{sk}_{y},\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{tk})\leftarrow\mathpzc{KG}(\mathsf{msk},y)
where
y
is a string. We denote the register holding

\mathpzc{sk}_{y}
as
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SK}}}
. Let
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{KT}}}
be a register that is
initialized to
\ket{0}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{KT}}}}
. If we apply the map

\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SK}}}}\ket{\beta}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{KT}}}}\rightarrow\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SK}}}}\ket{\beta\oplus\KeyTest(\tk,u)}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{KT}}}}

to the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SK}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{KT}}}
and then measure

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{KT}}}
in the computational basis, we obtain
1
with
overwhelming probability.

•

Security:
Consider the following experiment

\mathsf{Exp}_{\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{key}\mbox{-}\mathsf{test}}(1^{\lambda})
.

1.

The challenger
\mathpzc{Ch}
runs
\mathsf{msk}\leftarrow\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
and
initializes
\mathpzc{A}
with input
\mathsf{msk}
.

2.

\mathpzc{A}
can get access to the following oracle, where the list
L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
used by the oracles is initialized
to an empty list.

O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}(y)
:

Given
y
, it finds an entry of the form

(y,\mathsf{tk})
from
L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
. If there is such an entry, it
returns
\bot
.

Otherwise, it generates
(\mathpzc{sk}_{y},\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{tk})\leftarrow\mathpzc{KG}(\mathsf{msk},y)
, sends

(\mathpzc{sk}_{y},\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{tk})
to
\mathpzc{A}
, and adds
(y,\mathsf{tk})
to
L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
.

3.

\mathpzc{A}
sends a tuple of classical strings
(y,\mathsf{sk},x)
to
\mathpzc{Ch}
.

\mathpzc{Ch}
outputs
\bot
if there is no entry of the form
(y,\mathsf{tk})
in
L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
for some
\mathsf{tk}
.
Otherwise,
\mathpzc{Ch}
generates
\mathsf{ct}\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{msk},x)
, and outputs
\top
if

\mathsf{KeyTest}(\mathsf{tk},\mathsf{sk})=1
and
\mathsf{CDec}(\mathsf{sk},\mathsf{ct})\neq F(x,y)
, and outputs
\bot
otherwise.

For all QPT
\mathpzc{A}
, the following must hold:

\displaystyle\mathsf{Adv}_{\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{key}\mbox{-}\mathsf{test}}(1^{\lambda})\coloneqq\Pr[\mathsf{Exp}_{\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{key}\mbox{-}\mathsf{test}}(1^{\lambda})\rightarrow\top]\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

(53)

Definition 7.7 (Selective Single-Ciphertext KLA Security).

We say that an SKFE-CR-SKL scheme
\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}
is selective single-ciphertext secure, if
it satisfies the following requirement, formalized from the
experiment
\mathsf{Exp}_{\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{sel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{1ct}\mbox{-}\mathsf{kla}}(1^{\lambda},\mathsf{coin})

between an adversary
\mathpzc{A}
and a challenger:

1.

Initialized with
1^{\lambda}
,
\mathpzc{A}
outputs
(x_{0}^{*},x_{1}^{*})
. The challenger
\mathpzc{Ch}
runs
\mathsf{msk}\leftarrow\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
.

2.

\mathpzc{A}
can get access to the following (stateful) oracles,
where the list
L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
used by the oracles is initialized
to an empty list:

O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}(y)
:

Given
y
, it finds an entry of the form

(y,\mathsf{vk},V)
from
L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
. If there is such an entry, it
returns
\bot
.

Otherwise, it generates
(\mathpzc{sk},\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{tk})\leftarrow\mathpzc{KG}(\mathsf{msk},y)
, sends

\mathpzc{sk}
and
\mathsf{tk}
to
\mathpzc{A}
, and adds
(y,\mathsf{vk},\bot)
to
L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
.

O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{Vrfy}}}(y,\widetilde{\mathpzc{sk}})
:

Given

(y,\widetilde{\mathpzc{sk}})
, it finds an entry
(y,\mathsf{vk},V)
from

L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
. (If there is no such entry, it returns
\bot
.) It
then runs
d\leftarrow\mathpzc{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\widetilde{\mathpzc{sk}})
and returns

d
to
\mathpzc{A}
. If
V=\bot
, it updates the entry into

(y,\mathsf{vk},d)
.

3.

\mathpzc{A}
requests the challenge ciphertext. If there exists
an entry
(y,\mathsf{vk},V)
in
L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
such that
F(x_{0}^{*},y)\neq F(x_{1}^{*},y)
and
V=\bot
,
\mathpzc{Ch}
outputs
0
as the
final output of this experiment. Otherwise,
\mathpzc{Ch}
generates

\mathsf{ct}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{msk},x_{\mathsf{coin}}^{*})
and sends
\mathsf{ct}^{*}
to
\mathpzc{A}
.

4.

\mathpzc{A}
continues to make queries to
O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
. However,
\mathpzc{A}
is not allowed to send
y
such that
F(x_{0}^{*},y)\neq F(x_{1}^{*},y)
to
O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
.

5.

\mathpzc{A}
outputs a guess
\mathsf{coin}^{\prime}
for
\mathsf{coin}
.
\mathpzc{Ch}

outputs
\mathsf{coin}^{\prime}
as the final output of the experiment.

For any QPT
\mathpzc{A}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathsf{Adv}_{\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{sel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{1ct}\mbox{-}\mathsf{kla}}(1^{\lambda})\coloneqq\absolutevalue{\Pr[\expc{\SKFESKL,\qA}{sel}{1ct}{kla} (1^\secp,0) \ra 1] - \Pr[\expc{\SKFESKL,\qA}{sel}{1ct}{kla} (1^\secp,1) \ra 1] }\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

(54)

In Appendix
C
, we prove the following theorem:

Theorem 7.8.

Assuming the existence of a BB84-based SKE-CD scheme and the existence
of OWFs, there exists a selective single-ciphertext KLA secure
SKFE-CR-SKL scheme satisfying the key-testability property.

7.2
Definitions of ABE-CR-SKL

In this section, we recall definitions of ABE-CR-SKL by Agrawal et al.
[
AKN+23
]
.

Definition 7.9 (ABE-CR-SKL).

An ABE-CR-SKL scheme
\mathsf{ABE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}
is a tuple of five algorithms
(\mathsf{Setup},\mathpzc{KG},\mathsf{Enc},\mathpzc{Dec},\allowbreak\mathpzc{Vrfy})
.
Below, let
\mathcal{X}=\{\mathcal{X}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda}
,
\mathcal{Y}=\{\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda}
, and
R=\{R_{\lambda}:\mathcal{X}_{\lambda}\times\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda}\to\{0,1\}\}_{\lambda}
be the ciphertext attribute space, the key attribute
space, and the associated relation of
\mathsf{ABE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}
, respectively. Let

\mathcal{M}
denote the message space.

\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})\rightarrow(\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{msk})
:

The setup algorithm takes a security parameter
1^{\lambda}
, and outputs a public key
\mathsf{pk}
and master secret key
\mathsf{msk}
.

\mathpzc{KG}(\mathsf{msk},y)\rightarrow(\mathpzc{sk}_{y},\mathsf{vk})
:

The key generation algorithm
takes a master secret key
\mathsf{msk}
and a key attribute
y\in\mathcal{Y}
, and outputs a user secret key
\mathpzc{sk}_{y}
and a verification key
\mathsf{vk}
.

\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{pk},x,\mathsf{m})\rightarrow\mathsf{ct}
:

The encryption algorithm takes a public key
\mathsf{pk}
, a ciphertext attribute
x\in\mathcal{X}
, and a plaintext
\mathsf{m}\in\mathcal{M}
, and outputs a ciphertext
\mathsf{ct}
.

\mathpzc{Dec}(\mathpzc{sk}_{y},\mathsf{ct})\rightarrow\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
:

The decryption algorithm
takes a user secret key
\mathpzc{sk}_{y}
and a ciphertext
\mathsf{ct}
. It outputs
a value
\mathsf{m}^{\prime}\in\{\bot\}\cup\mathcal{M}
.

\mathpzc{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\mathpzc{sk}^{\prime})\rightarrow\top/\bot
:

The verification algorithm takes a verification key
\mathsf{vk}
and a quantum state
\mathpzc{sk}^{\prime}
, and outputs
\top
or
\bot
.

Decryption correctness:

For every
x\in\mathcal{X}
and
y\in\mathcal{Y}

satisfying
R(x,y)=0
and
\mathsf{m}\in\mathcal{M}
, we have

\displaystyle\Pr\left[\mathpzc{Dec}(\mathpzc{sk}_{y},\mathsf{ct})\allowbreak=\mathsf{m}\ \middle|\begin{array}[]{ll}(\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{msk})\leftarrow\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})\\
(\mathpzc{sk}_{y},\mathsf{vk})\leftarrow\mathpzc{KG}(\mathsf{msk},y)\\
\mathsf{ct}\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{pk},x,\mathsf{m})\end{array}\right]\geq 1-{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

Verification correctness:

For every
y\in\mathcal{Y}
, we have

\displaystyle\Pr\left[\mathpzc{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\mathpzc{sk}_{y})=\top\ \middle|\begin{array}[]{ll}(\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{msk})\leftarrow\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})\\
(\mathpzc{sk}_{y},\mathsf{vk})\leftarrow\mathpzc{KG}(\mathsf{msk},y)\\
\end{array}\right]\geq 1-{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

Definition 7.10 (Adaptive IND-KLA Security).

We say that an ABE-CR-SKL scheme
\mathsf{ABE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}
for relation
R:\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\to\{0,1\}
is adaptively IND-KLA secure, if it satisfies the
following requirement, formalized from the experiment

\mathsf{Exp}_{\mathsf{ABE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{ada}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ind}\mbox{-}\mathsf{kla}}(1^{\lambda},\mathsf{coin})
between an
adversary
\mathpzc{A}
and a challenger
\mathpzc{Ch}
:

1.

At the beginning,
\mathpzc{Ch}
runs
(\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{msk})\leftarrow\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})

and initializes the list
L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
to be an empty set.
Throughout the experiment,
\mathpzc{A}
can access the following oracles.

O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}(y)
:

Given
y
, it finds an entry of the form
(y,\mathsf{vk},V)
from
L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
. If there is such an entry, it returns
\bot
.
Otherwise, it generates
(\mathpzc{sk}_{y},\mathsf{vk})\leftarrow\mathpzc{KG}(\mathsf{msk},y)
,
sends
\mathpzc{sk}_{y}
to
\mathpzc{A}
, and adds
(y,\mathsf{vk},\bot)
to
L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
.

O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{Vrfy}}}(y,\widetilde{\mathpzc{sk}})
:

Given
(y,\widetilde{\mathpzc{sk}})
, it finds an entry
(y,\mathsf{vk},V)
from
L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
. (If there is no such entry, it returns
\bot
.)
It then runs
d\coloneqq\mathpzc{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\widetilde{\mathpzc{sk}})
and returns
d
to
\mathpzc{A}
.
If
V=\bot
, it updates the entry into
(y,\mathsf{vk},d)
.

2.

When
\mathpzc{A}
sends
(x^{*},\mathsf{m}_{0},\mathsf{m}_{1})
to
\mathpzc{Ch}
,
it checks that for every entry
(y,\mathsf{vk},V)
in

L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
such that
R(x^{*},y)=0
, it holds
that
V=\top
. If so,

\mathpzc{Ch}
generates
\mathsf{ct}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{pk},x^{*},\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{coin}})
and
sends
\mathsf{ct}^{*}
to
\mathpzc{A}
. Otherwise, it outputs
0
.

3.

\mathpzc{A}
continues to make queries to
O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}(\cdot)
and
O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{Vrfy}}}(\cdot,\cdot)
.
However,
\mathpzc{A}
is not allowed to send a key attribute

y
such that
R(x^{*},y)=0
to
O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
.

4.

\mathpzc{A}
outputs a guess
\mathsf{coin}^{\prime}
for
\mathsf{coin}
.

\mathpzc{Ch}
outputs
\mathsf{coin}^{\prime}
as the final output of the experiment.

For any QPT
\mathpzc{A}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathsf{Adv}_{\mathsf{ABE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{ada}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ind}\mbox{-}\mathsf{kla}}(1^{\lambda})\coloneqq\absolutevalue{\Pr[\expc{\ABESKL,\qA}{ada}{ind}{kla} (1^\secp,0) \ra 1] - \Pr[\expc{\ABESKL,\qA}{ada}{ind}{kla} (1^\secp,1) \ra 1] }\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

(60)

Remark
7.11.

Although we can handle the situation where multiple keys for the same attribute
y
are generated using an index management such as
(y,1,vk_{1},V_{1})
,
(y,2,vk_{2},V_{2})
, we use the simplified definition as Agrawal et al.
[
AKN+23
]
did.

We also consider relaxed versions of the above security notion.

Definition 7.12 (Selective IND-KLA
Security).

We consider the same security
game as that for adaptive IND-KLA security except that the adversary

\mathpzc{A}
should declare its target
x^{*}
at the beginning of the game
(even before it is given
\mathsf{pk}
).

We then define the advantage

\mathsf{Adv}_{\mathsf{ABE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{sel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ind}\mbox{-}\mathsf{kla}}(1^{\lambda})
for the selective case
similarly. We say
\mathsf{ABE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}
is selectively IND-KLA secure if for any
QPT adversary
\mathpzc{A}
,
\mathsf{Adv}_{\mathsf{ABE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{sel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ind}\mbox{-}\mathsf{kla}}(1^{\lambda})
is
negligible.

7.3
Construction

We construct an ABE-CR-SKL scheme

\mathsf{ABE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}=\mathsf{ABE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.(\mathsf{Setup},\mathpzc{KG},\allowbreak\mathsf{Enc},\mathpzc{Dec},\mathpzc{Vrfy})
for the relation

R
with the message space
\mathcal{M}\coloneqq\{0,1\}^{\ell_{\mathsf{m}}}
using the following building blocks.

•

ABE scheme
\mathsf{ABE}=\mathsf{ABE}.(\mathsf{Setup},\mathsf{KG},\mathsf{Enc},\mathsf{Dec})
for the following relation
R^{\prime}
.

R^{\prime}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})
:

Interpret
x^{\prime}\coloneqq x\|C
and

y^{\prime}\coloneqq y\|z
, where
C
is a circuit. Then, output
0
if

R(x,y)=0
and
C(z)=\bot
, and otherwise output
1
.

•

Compute-and-Compare Obfuscation
\mathsf{CC}.\mathsf{Obf}
with the simulator
\mathsf{CC}.\mathsf{Sim}
.

•

SKFE-CR-SKL scheme with key-testability

\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}=\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\allowbreak(\mathsf{Setup},\mathpzc{KG},\mathsf{Enc},\mathpzc{Dec},\mathpzc{Vrfy},\allowbreak\mathsf{KeyTest})
for
the following functionality
F
. It also has the classical
decryption algorithm
\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{CDec}
.

F(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{y})
:

Interpret

\widetilde{x}\coloneqq x\|z
and
\widetilde{y}\coloneqq y
. Then, output
z

if
R(x,y)=0
, and otherwise output
\bot
.

The construction is as follows.

\mathsf{ABE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
:

•

Generate
(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{msk})\leftarrow\mathsf{ABE}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
.

•

Generate
\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{msk}\leftarrow\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
.

•

Output
\mathsf{pk}\coloneqq\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk}
and
\mathsf{msk}\coloneqq(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{msk},\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{msk})
.

\mathsf{ABE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{KG}(\mathsf{msk},y)
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{msk}=(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{msk},\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{msk})
.

•

Generate
(\mathsf{skfe}.\mathpzc{sk},\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{tk})\leftarrow\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{KG}(\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{msk},y)
. We denote the register holding
\mathsf{skfe}.\mathpzc{sk}
as
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK}}}
.

•

Sample explicit randomness
\mathsf{k}\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{\lambda}
.

•

Prepare a register
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}
that is initialized to
\ket{0\cdots 0}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}}
.

•

Apply the map

\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK}}}}\ket{v}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}}\rightarrow\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK}}}}\ket{v\oplus\ABE.\KG(\abe.\msk,y\|u,\key)}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}}
to the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}
, and obtain
\mathpzc{sk}
over the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}
.

•

Output
\mathpzc{sk}
and

\mathsf{vk}\coloneqq(y,\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{msk},\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{tk},\mathsf{k})
.

\mathsf{ABE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{pk},x,\mathsf{m})
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{pk}=\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk}
.

•

Generate
\widetilde{C}\leftarrow\mathsf{CC}.\mathsf{Sim}(1^{\lambda},\mathsf{pp}_{D},1)
, where
\mathsf{pp}_{D}
consists of circuit parameters of
D
defined in the security proof.

•

Generate
\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{ct}\leftarrow\mathsf{ABE}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk},x\|\widetilde{C},\mathsf{m})
.

•

Output
\mathsf{ct}\coloneqq\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{ct}
.

\mathsf{ABE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{Dec}(\mathpzc{sk},\mathsf{ct})
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{ct}=\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{ct}
. We denote the register holding
\mathpzc{sk}
as
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK}}}\otimes{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}
.

•

Prepare a register
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{MSG}}}
that is initialized to
\ket{0\cdots 0}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{MSG}}}}

•

Apply the map
\ket{v}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}}\ket{w}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{MSG}}}}\rightarrow\ket{v}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}}\ket{w\oplus\ABE.\Dec(v,\abe.\ct)}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{MSG}}}}
to the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{MSG}}}
.

•

Measure the register
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{MSG}}}
in the computational basis and output the result
\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
.

\mathsf{ABE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\mathpzc{sk}^{\prime})
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{vk}=(y,\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{msk},\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{tk},\mathsf{k})
. We denote the register holding
\mathpzc{sk}^{\prime}
as
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK}}}\otimes{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}
.

•

Prepare a register
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.KT}}}
that is initialized to
\ket{0}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.KT}}}}
.

•

Apply the map
\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK}}}}\ket{\beta}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.KT}}}}\rightarrow\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK}}}}\ket{\beta\oplus\SKFESKL.\KeyTest(\skfe.\tk,u)}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.KT}}}}
to the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.KT}}}
.

•

Measure
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.KT}}}
in the computational basis and
output
\bot
if the result is
0
. Otherwise, go to the next step.

•

Apply the map

\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK}}}}\ket{v}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}}\rightarrow\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK}}}}\ket{v\oplus\ABE.\KG(\abe.\msk,y\|u,\key)}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}}
to the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}
.

•

Trace out the register
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}
and obtain

\mathsf{skfe}.\mathpzc{sk}^{\prime}
over register
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK}}}
.

•

Output
\top
if
\top=\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{Vrfy}(\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{skfe}.\mathpzc{sk}^{\prime})
and
\bot
otherwise.

Decryption correctness:

Let
x
and
y
be a ciphertext-attribute and a key-attribute,
respectively, such that
R(x,y)=0
. The secret-key

\mathpzc{sk}
output by
\mathsf{ABE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{KG}
is of the form
\sum_{u}\alpha_{u}\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK}}}}\ket{\abe.\sk_{y\|u}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}}
,
where
\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{sk}_{y\|u}\leftarrow\mathsf{ABE}.\mathsf{KG}(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{msk},y\|u,\mathsf{k})
.
Let
\mathsf{ct}\leftarrow\mathsf{ABE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{pk},x,\mathsf{m})
, where

\mathsf{ct}=\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{ct}\leftarrow\mathsf{ABE}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk},x\|\widetilde{C},\mathsf{m})
and

\widetilde{C}\leftarrow\mathsf{CC}.\mathsf{Sim}(1^{\lambda},\mathsf{pp}_{D},1)
.
If we apply the map

\displaystyle\ket{v}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}}\ket{w}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{MSG}}}}\rightarrow\ket{v}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}}\ket{w\oplus\ABE.\Dec(v,\abe.\ct)}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{MSG}}}}

(61)

to
\sum_{u}\alpha_{u}\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK}}}}\ket{\abe.\sk_{y\|u}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}}\otimes\ket{0\cdots 0}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{MSG}}}}
, the result is

\displaystyle\sum_{u}\alpha_{u}\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK}}}}\ket{\abe.\sk_{y\|u}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK}}}}\otimes\ket{\msg}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{MSG}}}}

(62)

since
\widetilde{C}(u)=\bot
and thus
R^{\prime}(x\|\widetilde{C},y\|u)=0
for every string
u
.
Therefore,
\mathsf{ABE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}
satisfies decryption correctness.

Verification correctness.

Let
y
be a key attribute and
(\mathpzc{sk},\mathsf{vk})\leftarrow\mathsf{ABE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{KG}(\mathsf{msk},y)
.
It is clear that the state
\mathsf{skfe}.\mathpzc{sk}^{\prime}
obtained when computing
\mathsf{ABE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\mathpzc{sk})
is the same as
\mathsf{skfe}.\mathpzc{sk}
generated when generating
\mathpzc{sk}
.
Therefore, the verification correctness of
\mathsf{ABE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}
follows from that of
\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}
.

7.4
Proof of Selective IND-KLA Security

Let
\mathpzc{A}
be an adversary for the selective IND-KLA security of
\mathsf{ABE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}
.
We consider the following sequence of experiments.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}^{\mathsf{coin}}
:

This is
\mathsf{Exp}_{\mathsf{ABE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{sel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ind}\mbox{-}\mathsf{kla}}(1^{\lambda},\mathsf{coin})
.

1.

\mathpzc{A}
declares the challenge ciphertext attribute
x^{*}
. The
challenger
\mathpzc{Ch}
generates
(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{msk})\leftarrow\mathsf{ABE}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
and
\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{msk}\leftarrow\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
, and sends
\mathsf{pk}\coloneqq\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk}
to
\mathpzc{A}
.

2.

\mathpzc{A}
can get access to the following (stateful) oracles, where the list
L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
used by the oracles is initialized to an empty list:

O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}(y)
:

Given
y
, it finds an entry of the form

(y,\mathsf{vk}_{y},V)
from
L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
. If there is such an entry, it
returns
\bot
. Otherwise, it generates
\mathpzc{sk}_{y},\mathsf{vk}_{y}
as follows.

•

Generate
(\mathsf{skfe}.\mathpzc{sk}_{y},\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{vk}_{y},\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{tk}_{y})\leftarrow\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{KG}(\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{msk},y)
. We denote the register holding
\mathsf{skfe}.\mathpzc{sk}_{y}
as
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK_{y}}}}
.

•

Prepare a register
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{y}}}}
that is initialized to
\ket{0\cdots 0}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{y}}}}}
.

•

Sample explicit randomness
\mathsf{k}_{y}\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{\lambda}
.

•

Apply the map

\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK_{y}}}}}\ket{v}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{y}}}}}\rightarrow\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK_{y}}}}}\ket{v\oplus\ABE.\KG(\abe.\msk,y\|u,\key_y)}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{y}}}}}
to the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK_{y}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{y}}}}
, and obtain
\mathpzc{sk}_{y}
over the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK_{y}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{y}}}}
.

•

Set
\mathsf{vk}_{y}\coloneqq(y,\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{msk},\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{vk}_{y},\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{tk}_{y},\mathsf{k}_{y})
.

It returns
\mathpzc{sk}_{y}
to
\mathpzc{A}
and adds the entry
(y,\mathsf{vk}_{y},\bot)
to
L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
.

O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{Vrfy}}}(y,\widetilde{\mathpzc{sk}})
:

It finds an entry

(y,\mathsf{vk}_{y},V)
from
L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
. (If there is no such entry, it
returns
\bot
.) It parses

\mathsf{vk}_{y}=(y,\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{msk},\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{vk}_{y},\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{tk}_{y},\mathsf{k}_{y})
and computes

d
as follows.

•

Let the register holding
\widetilde{\mathpzc{sk}}
be
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK_{y}}}}\otimes{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{y}}}}
.

•

Prepare a register
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.KT_{y}}}}
that is initialized to
\ket{0}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.KT_{y}}}}}
.

•

Apply
\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK_{y}}}}}\ket{\beta}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.KT_{y}}}}}\rightarrow\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK_{y}}}}}\ket{\beta\oplus\SKFESKL.\KeyTest(\skfe.\tk_y,u)}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.KT_{y}}}}}
to the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK_{y}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.KT_{y}}}}
.

•

Measure
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKE.KT_{y}}}}
in the computational basis and
set
d\coloneqq\bot
if the result is
0
. Otherwise, go to the next step.

•

Apply the map

\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK_{y}}}}}\ket{v}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{y}}}}}\rightarrow\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK_{y}}}}}\ket{v\oplus\ABE.\KG(\abe.\msk,y\|u,\key_y)}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{y}}}}}
to the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK_{y}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{y}}}}
.

•

Trace out the register
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{y}}}}
and obtain
\mathsf{skfe}.\mathpzc{sk}^{\prime}
over
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK_{y}}}}
.

•

Set
d\coloneqq\top
if
\top=\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{Vrfy}(\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{vk}_{y},\mathsf{skfe}.\mathpzc{sk}^{\prime})
and set
d\coloneqq\bot
otherwise.
It returns
d
to
\mathpzc{A}
. Finally, if
V=\bot
, it updates the entry

(y,\mathsf{vk}_{y},V)
to
(y,\mathsf{vk}_{y},d)
.

3.

\mathpzc{A}
sends
(\mathsf{m}_{0}^{*},\mathsf{m}_{1}^{*})\in\mathcal{M}^{2}
to

\mathpzc{Ch}
.
\mathpzc{Ch}
checks if for every entry
(y,\mathsf{vk}_{y},V)
in

L_{\mathpzc{KG}}
such that
R(x^{*},y)=0
, it holds that
V=\top
. If
so, it generates
\widetilde{C}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{CC}.\mathsf{Sim}(1^{\lambda},\mathsf{pp}_{D},1)
and

\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{ABE}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk},x^{*}\|\widetilde{C}^{*},\allowbreak\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{coin}}^{*})
, and
sends
\mathsf{ct}^{*}\coloneqq\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}
to
\mathpzc{A}
. Otherwise, it outputs
0
.

4.

\mathpzc{A}
outputs
\mathsf{coin}^{\prime}
. The challenger outputs
\mathsf{coin}^{\prime}

as the final output of the experiment.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}^{\mathsf{coin}}
:

This is the same as
\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}^{\mathsf{coin}}
except
that
\widetilde{C}^{*}
is generated as

\widetilde{C}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{CC}.\mathsf{Obf}(1^{\lambda},D[\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}],\mathsf{lock},0)
, where
\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{msk},\allowbreak x^{*}\|0^{\lambda})
,
\mathsf{lock}\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{\lambda}
, and
D[\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}](x)
is a circuit that has
\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}
hardwired and outputs
\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{CDec}(x,\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{ct}^{*})
.

We pick
\mathsf{lock}
as a uniformly random string that is completely independent of other variables such as
\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}
.
Thus, from the security of
\mathsf{CC}.\mathsf{Obf}
, we have
\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hybi{0}^\coin=1]-\Pr[\hybi{1}^\coin=1]}={\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda)
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}^{\mathsf{coin}}
:

This is the same as
\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}^{\mathsf{coin}}
except
that
\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}
hardwired into the circuit
D
is generated
as
\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{msk},x^{*}\|\mathsf{lock})
.

From the selective single-ciphertext security of
\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}
, we can show that

\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}^{\mathsf{coin}}\approx\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}^{\mathsf{coin}}
. Suppose that
\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}^{\mathsf{coin}}\not\approx\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}^{\mathsf{coin}}
and
\mathpzc{D}
is a corresponding
distinguisher. We consider the following reduction

\mathpzc{R}
:

Execution of
\mathpzc{R}^{\mathpzc{D}}
in

\mathsf{Exp}_{\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL},\mathpzc{R}}^{\mathsf{sel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{1ct}\mbox{-}\mathsf{kla}}(1^{\lambda},b)
:

1.

\mathpzc{Ch}
runs
\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{msk}\leftarrow\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
and initializes
\mathpzc{R}

with the security parameter
1^{\lambda}
.

2.

\mathpzc{D}
declares the challenge ciphertext-attribute
x^{*}
.
\mathpzc{R}

generates
(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{msk})\leftarrow\mathsf{ABE}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
and
sends
\mathsf{ek}\coloneqq\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk}
to
\mathpzc{D}
.

3.

\mathpzc{R}
chooses
\mathsf{lock}\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{\lambda}
and sends
(\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{m}_{0}^{*},\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{m}_{1}^{*})\coloneqq(x^{*}\|0^{\lambda},x^{*}\|\mathsf{lock})
to
\mathpzc{Ch}
.

4.

\mathpzc{R}
simulates the access to
O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}(y)
for
\mathpzc{D}
as
follows, where
L_{\mathpzc{KG}}
is a list initialized to be empty:

O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}(y):
Given
y
, it finds an entry of the
form
(y,\mathsf{vk}_{y},V)
from
L_{\mathpzc{KG}}
. If there is such an entry,
it returns
\bot
. Otherwise, it generates
(\mathpzc{sk}_{y},\mathsf{vk}_{y})

similar to
\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}^{\mathsf{coin}}
except that the values

(\mathsf{skfe}.\mathpzc{sk}_{y},\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{vk}_{y},\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{tk}_{y})
are generated as

(\mathsf{skfe}.\mathpzc{sk}_{y},\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{vk}_{y},\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{tk}_{y})\leftarrow\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}(y)
instead.
It adds
(y,\mathsf{vk}_{y},\bot)
to
L_{\mathpzc{KG}}
and returns
\mathpzc{sk}_{y}
.

5.

\mathpzc{R}
simulates the access to
O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{Vrfy}}}(y,\widetilde{\mathpzc{sk}})
as follows:

O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{Vrfy}}}(y,\widetilde{\mathpzc{sk}}):
Given
(y,\widetilde{\mathpzc{sk}})
, it finds an entry
(y,\mathsf{vk}_{y},V)
from

L_{\mathpzc{KG}}
(If there is no such entry, it returns
\perp
). It then
executes a procedure similar to that in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}^{\mathsf{coin}}
, except that
\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{Vrfy}}}(y,\mathsf{skfe}.\mathpzc{sk}^{\prime})
is executed instead of
\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{Vrfy}(\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{vk}_{y},\mathsf{skfe}.\mathpzc{sk}^{\prime})
. The corresponding output
d
is returned as the
output of the oracle.

6.

\mathpzc{R}
requests the challenge ciphertext from
\mathpzc{Ch}
and receives

\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{msk},\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{m}^{*}_{b})
.

7.

\mathpzc{D}
sends
(\mathsf{m}_{0}^{*},\mathsf{m}_{1}^{*})\in\mathcal{M}^{2}
to
\mathpzc{R}
.
\mathpzc{R}

checks that for every entry
(y,\mathsf{vk}_{y},V)
such that
R(x^{*},y)=0
, it
holds that
V=\top
. If so, it
generates
\widetilde{C}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{CC}.\mathsf{Obf}(1^{\lambda},D[\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}],\mathsf{lock},0)

and
\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{ABE}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk},x^{*}\|\widetilde{C}^{*},\mathsf{m}^{*}_{\mathsf{coin}})
and
sends
\mathsf{ct}^{*}\coloneqq\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}
to
\mathpzc{D}
. Else, it outputs
0
.

8.

\mathpzc{D}
outputs a bit
b^{\prime}
.
\mathpzc{R}
outputs
b^{\prime}
and
\mathpzc{Ch}

outputs
b^{\prime}
as the final output of the experiment.

It is easy to see that the view of
\mathpzc{D}
is the same as that in

\mathsf{Hyb}^{\mathsf{coin}}_{2}
when
\mathsf{lock}
is encrypted in
\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}
and that
of
\mathsf{Hyb}^{\mathsf{coin}}_{1}
when
0^{\lambda}
is encrypted. Moreover, for
\mathpzc{D}

to distinguish between the two hybrids, it must be the case that

V=\top
for all entries
(y,\mathsf{vk}_{y},V)
such that
R(x^{*},y)=0
, which directly implies that the analogous values checked by

\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{Vrfy}}}
must also be
\top
. If
R(x^{*},y)=1
,
F(x^{*}\|0^{\lambda},y)=F(x^{*}\|\mathsf{lock},y)=\bot
. Consequently,

\mathpzc{R}
breaks the selective single-ciphertext security of
\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}
. Therefore, it
must be that
\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}^{\mathsf{coin}}\approx\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}^{\mathsf{coin}}
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}^{\mathsf{coin}}
:

This is the same as
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2}^{\mathsf{coin}}
except that
O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
behaves as follows. (The difference is red colored.)

O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}(y)
:

Given
y
, it finds an entry of the form

(y,\mathsf{vk}_{y},V)
from
L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
. If there is such an entry, it
returns
\bot
. Otherwise, it generates
\mathpzc{sk}_{y},\mathsf{vk}_{y}
as follows.

•

Generate
(\mathsf{skfe}.\mathpzc{sk}_{y},\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{vk}_{y},\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{tk}_{y})\leftarrow\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{KG}(\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{msk},y)
. We denote the register holding
\mathsf{skfe}.\mathpzc{sk}_{y}
as
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK_{y}}}}
.

•

Prepare a register
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.R^{\prime}_{y}}}}
that is initialized to
\ket{0}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.R^{\prime}_{y}}}}}
.

•

Apply the map
\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK_{y}}}}}\ket{\beta}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.R^{\prime}_{y}}}}}\rightarrow\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK_{y}}}}}\ket{\beta\oplus R^\prime(x^*\|\tlC^*,y\|u)}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.R^{\prime}_{y}}}}}
to the registers

and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.R^{\prime}_{y}}}}
.

Measure
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.R^{\prime}_{y}}}}
in the computational basis
and output
\bot
if the result is
0
. Otherwise, go to the next step.

•

Prepare a register
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{y}}}}
that is initialized to
\ket{0\cdots 0}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{y}}}}}
.

•

Sample explicit randomness
\mathsf{k}_{y}\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{\lambda}
.

•

Apply the map

\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK_{y}}}}}\ket{v}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{y}}}}}\rightarrow\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK_{y}}}}}\ket{v\oplus\ABE.\KG(\abe.\msk,y\|u,\key_y)}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{y}}}}}
to the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK_{y}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{y}}}}
, and obtain
\mathpzc{sk}_{y}
over the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK_{y}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{y}}}}
.

•

Set
\mathsf{vk}_{y}\coloneqq(y,\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{msk},\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{vk}_{y},\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{tk}_{y},\mathsf{k}_{y})
.

It returns
\mathpzc{sk}_{y}
to
\mathpzc{A}
and adds the entry
(y,\mathsf{vk}_{y},\bot)
to
L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
.

With overwhelming probability, we observe that the added procedure that checks

R^{\prime}(x^{*}\|\widetilde{C}^{*},y\|u)
in superposition affects the final state
\mathpzc{sk}_{y}
at most negligibly.
We consider the following two cases.

•

The first case is where
R(x^{*},y)=1
. In this case, we have

R^{\prime}(x^{*}\|\widetilde{C}^{*},y\|u)=1
for any
u
.
Hence, we see that the added procedure does not affect the state
\mathsf{skfe}.\mathpzc{sk}_{y}
.

•

The second case is where
R(x^{*},y)=0
. Let

\mathsf{skfe}.\mathpzc{sk}_{y}=\sum_{u}\alpha_{u}\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKFE.SK_{y}}}}}
. From
the classical decryption property of
\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}
, we have that for
any
u
, it holds that

\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{CDec}(u,\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{ct}^{*})=\mathsf{lock}
. Thus,
\widetilde{C}^{*}(u)\neq\bot
and

R^{\prime}(x^{*}\|\widetilde{C}^{*},y\|u)=1
with overwhelming probability from
the correctness of
\mathsf{CC}.\mathsf{Obf}
. Hence, we see that the added procedure affects the state
\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{sk}_{y}
at most negligibly.

Therefore, we have
\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hybi{2}^\coin=1]-\Pr[\hybi{3}^\coin=1]}={\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda)
.

\underline{\mathpzc{A}_{\mathsf{o2h}}^{\widetilde{O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}},\mathcal{O}}(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{msk})}:

\mathpzc{D}

.

, it holds that

. If so, it generates

, where

to

.

.

6.

\mathpzc{D}
outputs a guess

.
\mathpzc{A}_{\mathsf{o2h}}
outputs
b^{\prime}
.

Let
H
be an oracle that for every input

, outputs
1
.
Consider now the extractor
\mathpzc{B}_{\mathsf{o2h}}^{\widetilde{O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}},H}

as specified by the O2H Lemma (Lemma
3.2
). We will now
construct a reduction

that runs

by simulating the oracles

and

for
\mathpzc{B}_{\mathsf{o2h}}
, and breaks

Execution of
\mathpzc{R}
in

\mathsf{Exp}_{\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL},\mathpzc{R}}^{\mathsf{key}\mbox{-}\mathsf{test}}(1^{\lambda})
:

1.

The challenger

and
initializes
\mathpzc{R}
with input
\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{msk}
.

samples

and initializes
\mathpzc{B}_{\mathsf{o2h}}
with the input

(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{msk})
.

\mathpzc{R}
simulates the access to
\widetilde{O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}}(y)
for
\mathpzc{B}
as
follows, where
L_{\mathpzc{KG}}
is a list initialized to be empty:

\widetilde{O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}}(y):
Given
y
, it finds an entry of the
form
(y,\mathsf{vk}_{y},V)
from
L_{\mathpzc{KG}}
(If there is such an entry,
it returns
(\bot,L_{\mathpzc{KG}})
). Otherwise, it generates

similar to
\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}^{\mathsf{coin}}
.
It adds
(y,\mathsf{vk}_{y},\bot)
to
L_{\mathpzc{KG}}
and returns

.

4.

When
\mathpzc{B}_{\mathsf{o2h}}
queries an input
u
to

,
\mathpzc{R}
responds with
1
.

\mathpzc{B}_{\mathsf{o2h}}
outputs values
y
and
\mathsf{sk}
.
\mathpzc{R}
sends
(\mathsf{sk},y,\mathsf{lock})
to
\mathpzc{Ch}
.

We will now claim that with non-negligible probability,
\mathpzc{R}
obtains
values
\mathsf{sk}
and
y
such that
R^{\prime}(x^{*}\|\widetilde{C}^{*},y\|\mathsf{sk})=0
. Recall that
the hybrids differ only when
R(x^{*},y)=0
. By the definition of

R^{\prime}
and
\widetilde{C}^{*}
and the decryption correctness of
\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}
, this
will imply that
\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{CDec}(\mathsf{sk},\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{ct}^{\star})\neq F(x^{*}\|\mathsf{lock},y)
since
F(x^{*}\|\mathsf{lock},y)=\mathsf{lock}
, and
R^{\prime}(x^{*}\|\widetilde{C}^{*},y\|\mathsf{sk})=0
requires
\widetilde{C}^{*}(\mathsf{sk})=\bot
. Moreover,

\mathsf{KeyTest}(\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{tk}_{y},\mathsf{sk})
also holds. Consequently, this will imply

\mathpzc{R}
breaks the key-testability of
\mathsf{SKFE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}
. To prove this, we will
rely on the One-Way to Hiding (O2H) Lemma (Lemma
3.2
).
Consider an oracle
G
which takes as input
u
and outputs
R^{\prime}(x^{*}\|\widetilde{C}^{\star},y\|u)
and an oracle
H
which takes as input
u
and
outputs
1
. Notice that if the oracle
\mathcal{O}=G
, then the view of

\mathpzc{D}
as run by
\mathpzc{A}_{\mathsf{o2h}}
is the same as in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{4}^{\mathsf{coin}}
, while if
\mathcal{O}=H
, the view
of
\mathpzc{D}
is the same as in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}^{\mathsf{coin}}
. By the O2H Lemma, we
have the following, where
z=(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{skfe}.\mathsf{msk})
.

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\Pr[\qA_\oh^{\widetilde{\Oracle{\qKG}},
H}(z)=1]-\Pr[\qA_\oh^{\widetilde{\Oracle{\qKG}}, G}(z)=1]}\leq 2q\cdot\sqrt{\Pr[\mathpzc{B}_{\mathsf{o2h}}^{\widetilde{O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}},H}(z)\in S]}\kern 5.0pt.

(63)

where
S
is a set where
the oracles
H
and
G
differ, which happens only for inputs
u
s.t.

R^{\prime}(x^{*}\|\widetilde{C}^{\star},y\|u)=0
. Since
\mathpzc{R}
obtains
\mathsf{sk}
and
y
as the
output of
\mathpzc{B}_{\mathsf{o2h}}^{\widetilde{O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}},H}(z)
, the argument
follows that
\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}^{\mathsf{coin}}\approx\mathsf{Hyb}_{4}^{\mathsf{coin}}
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{5}^{\mathsf{coin}}
:

This is the same as
\mathsf{Hyb}_{4}^{\mathsf{coin}}
except that
\mathsf{ct}^{*}\coloneqq\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}
is generated as
\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{ct}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{ABE}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk},x^{*}\|\widetilde{C}^{*},0^{\ell_{\mathsf{m}}})
.

The view of
\mathpzc{A}
in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{4}^{\mathsf{coin}}
and
\mathsf{Hyb}_{5}^{\mathsf{coin}}
can be simulated with
\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk}
and the access to the quantum key generation oracle
O_{\mathtt{qkg}}
.
This is because before
\mathsf{ABE}.\mathsf{KG}
is required to be applied in the
simulation of oracles
O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
and
O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{Vrfy}}}
, the
relation check
R^{\prime}(x^{*}\|\widetilde{C}^{\star},y\|u)
is already applied in
superposition.
Thus, we have
\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hybi{4}^\coin=1]-\Pr[\hybi{5}^\coin=1]}={\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda)
.

Lastly,
\mathsf{Hyb}_{5}^{0}
and
\mathsf{Hyb}_{5}^{1}
are exactly the same experiment and thus we have
\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hybi{5}^0=1]-\Pr[\hybi{5}^1=1]}={\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda)
.
Then, from the above arguments, we obtain

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\Pr[\expc{\ABESKL,\qA}{sel}{ind}{kla}(1^\secp,0)=1]-\expc{\ABESKL,\qA}{sel}{ind}{kla}(1^\secp,1)=1]}

(64)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\Pr[\hybi{0}^0=1]-\Pr[\hybi{0}^1=1]}\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

(65)

This completes the proof. ∎

Recall now that SKFE-CR-SKL with Key-Testability is implied by
BB84-based SKE-CD, OWFs and adaptively single-ciphertext
function-private SKFE. Since this notion of SKFE is implied by OWFs
(Theorem
7.3
), we have that
SKFE-CR-SKL with Key-Testability is implied by LWE. Since,
Compute-and-Compare Obfuscation and quantum-secure Ciphertext-Policy
ABE for Circuits are both implied by LWE, this gives us the
following theorem:

Theorem 7.13.

There exists an ABE-CR-SKL scheme satisfying Selective IND-KLA
security, assuming the polynomial hardness of the LWE assumption.

8
ABE-CR
2
-SKL from Multi-Input ABE

In this section, we show how to achieve ABE-CR-SKL with classical certificates (ABE-CR
2
-SKL) from MI-ABE.

8.1
Definitions of Multi-Input ABE

First, we recall the definitions of MI-ABE.
The syntax of MI-ABE is as follows:

Definition 8.1.

A Multi-Input (Ciphertext-Policy) Attribute-Based Encryption scheme

\mathsf{MI}\textrm{-}\mathsf{ABE}
is a tuple of four PPT algorithms
(\mathsf{Setup},\mathsf{KeyGen},\allowbreak\mathsf{Enc},\mathsf{Dec})
. Let
k={\mathrm{poly}}(\lambda)
and let
\{\mathcal{X}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda}
,

\{(\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda})^{k}\}_{\lambda}
and
R=\{R_{\lambda}:\mathcal{X}_{\lambda}\times(\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda})^{k}\rightarrow\{0,1\}\}
be the ciphertext attribute space, key
attribute space and the relation associated with
\mathsf{MI}\textrm{-}\mathsf{ABE}

respectively. Let
\{0,1\}^{\ell}
be the message space. Let
s(\lambda)

denote the maximum bit string length required to describe PPT
circuits with input size
k\cdot n(\lambda)
and depth
d(\lambda)
for
polynomials
s,n
and
d
. Let
\mathcal{X}_{\lambda}=\{0,1\}^{s(\lambda)}
and

\mathcal{Y}=\{0,1\}^{n(\lambda)}
. Let
R
be such that
R(x,y_{1},\ldots,y_{k})=0\iff x(y_{1},\ldots,y_{k})=\bot
where
x\in\mathcal{X}
is
parsed as a
k
-input circuit and
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{k})\in\mathcal{Y}^{k}
.

\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})\rightarrow(\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{msk})
:

The setup algorithm takes a security parameter
1^{\lambda}
and outputs a public key
\mathsf{pk}
and master secret key
\mathsf{msk}
.

\mathsf{KG}(\mathsf{msk},i,y_{i})\rightarrow\mathsf{sk}_{i}
:

The key generation algorithm
takes the master secret key
\mathsf{msk}
, an index
i\in[k]
, and a
key-attribute
y_{i}\in\mathcal{Y}
as input. It outputs a secret-key

\mathsf{sk}_{i}
.

\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{pk},x,\mathsf{m})\rightarrow\mathsf{ct}
:

The encryption algorithm takes
the public key
\mathsf{pk}
, a ciphertext attribute
x\in\mathcal{X}
, and a
message
\mathsf{m}\in\{0,1\}^{\ell}
as input. It outputs a ciphertext

\mathsf{ct}
.

\mathsf{Dec}(\mathsf{ct},\mathsf{sk}_{1},\ldots\mathsf{sk}_{k})\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathsf{m}}
:

The decryption algorithm takes as input a ciphertext
\mathsf{ct}
and
k

secret-keys
\mathsf{sk}_{1},\ldots,\mathsf{sk}_{k}
. It outputs a value

\widetilde{\mathsf{m}}\in\{0,1\}^{\ell}\cup\bot
.

Correctness:

We require that

\Pr\left[\mathsf{Dec}(\mathsf{ct},\mathsf{sk}_{1},\ldots,\mathsf{sk}_{k})=\mathsf{m}\ \Bigg\lvert\begin{array}[]{rl}&(\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{msk})\leftarrow\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda}),\\
&\forall i\in[k]:\mathsf{sk}_{i}\leftarrow\mathsf{KG}(\mathsf{msk},i,y_{i}),\\
&\mathsf{ct}\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{pk},x,\mathsf{m})\end{array}\right]\geq 1-{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

(66)

holds for all
x\in\mathcal{X}
and
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{k})\in\mathcal{Y}^{k}
such
that
R(x,y_{1},\ldots,y_{k})=0
and
\mathsf{m}\in\{0,1\}^{\ell}
.

Remark
8.2.

We say that an MI-ABE scheme has
polynomial-arity
, if it
allows
k
to be an arbitrary polynomial in
\lambda
.

Definition 8.3 (Post-Quantum Selective Security for MI-ABE:).

We say that
\mathsf{MI}\textrm{-}\mathsf{ABE}
is a selective-secure MI-ABE scheme for relation

R:\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}^{k}\rightarrow\{0,1\}
, if it satisfies the following
requirement, formalized from the experiment

\mathsf{Exp}_{\mathsf{MI}\textrm{-}\mathsf{ABE},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{sel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ind}}(1^{\lambda},\mathsf{coin})
between an adversary

\mathpzc{A}
and a challenger
\mathsf{Ch}
:

1.

\mathpzc{A}
declares the challenge ciphertext attribute

x
.
\mathsf{Ch}
runs
(\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{msk})\leftarrow\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
and sends
\mathsf{pk}
to
\mathpzc{A}
.

2.

\mathpzc{A}
can get access to the following key generation oracle.

O_{\mathtt{kg}}(i,y_{i})
:

It outputs
\mathsf{sk}_{i}\leftarrow\mathsf{KG}(\mathsf{msk},i,y_{i})
to
\mathpzc{A}
.

3.

At some point,
\mathpzc{A}
sends
(\mathsf{m}_{0},\mathsf{m}_{1})
to
\mathsf{Ch}
. Then,
\mathsf{Ch}

generates
\mathsf{ct}^{*}\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{pk},x,\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{coin}})
and sends
\mathsf{ct}^{*}
to

\mathpzc{A}
.

4.

Again,
\mathpzc{A}
can get access to the oracle
O_{\mathtt{kg}}
.

5.

\mathpzc{A}
outputs a guess
\mathsf{coin}^{\prime}
for
\mathsf{coin}
and the
experiment outputs
\mathsf{coin}^{\prime}
.

For an adversary to be admissible, we require that for all tuples

(y_{1},\ldots,y_{k})\in\mathcal{Y}^{k}
received by
\mathpzc{A}
, it must hold that

R(x,y_{1},\ldots,y_{k})=1
(non-decrytable). Given this
constraint, we say
\mathsf{MI}\textrm{-}\mathsf{ABE}
satisfies selective security if for all
QPT
\mathpzc{A}
, the following holds:

\displaystyle\mathsf{Adv}_{\mathsf{MI}\textrm{-}\mathsf{ABE},\mathpzc{A}}^{\mathsf{sel}\mbox{-}\mathsf{ind}}(1^{\lambda})\coloneqq\absolutevalue{\Pr[\expb{\MIABE,\qA}{sel}{ind} (1^\secp,0) \ra 1] -
\Pr[\expb{\MIABE,\qA}{sel}{ind} (1^\secp,1) \ra 1] }\leq{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

(67)

Remark
8.4.

We do not require quantum security in
Definition

8.3
unlike
Definition

3.5
.

Comparison with the Definition of Agrawal et.
al
[
ARYY23
]
.

Their definition considers a key-policy variant of MI-ABE. It
consists of algorithms
(\mathsf{Setup},\mathsf{Enc},\mathsf{KG}_{1},\cdots,\allowbreak\mathsf{KG}_{k-1},\mathsf{KG}_{k},\mathsf{Dec})
where
\mathsf{Enc}
works in a similar way
as our definition, i.e.,
\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{pk},\allowbreak x_{0},\mathsf{m})\rightarrow\mathsf{ct}
for message

\mathsf{m}
and attribute
x_{0}
. For each
i\in[k-1]
,
\mathsf{KG}_{i}
works
as
\mathsf{KG}_{i}(\mathsf{msk},x_{i})\rightarrow\mathsf{sk}_{i}
for the
i
-th key-attribute

x_{i}
. On the other hand,
\mathsf{KG}_{k}
works as
\mathsf{KG}_{k}(\mathsf{msk},f)\rightarrow\mathsf{sk}_{f}
where
f
is an arbitrary
k
-input function. The
guarantee is that decryption is feasible if and only if

f(x_{0},\ldots,x_{k-1})=\bot
. The algorithm
\mathsf{Dec}
has the
syntax
\mathsf{Dec}(\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{ct},\mathsf{sk}_{1},\ldots,\mathsf{sk}_{k-1},\mathsf{sk}_{f})\rightarrow\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
.

It is easy to see that this definition directly implies a

(k-1)
-input ciphertext-policy MI-ABE with algorithms
(\mathsf{Setup}^{\prime},\mathsf{Enc}^{\prime},\mathsf{KG}^{\prime},\mathsf{Dec}^{\prime})
. Specifically,
\mathsf{Setup}^{\prime}(1^{\lambda})\coloneqq\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
,
\mathsf{Enc}^{\prime}(\mathsf{pk},x_{0},\mathsf{m})\coloneqq\big(\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{pk},x_{0},\mathsf{m})\big)
and
\mathsf{KG}^{\prime}
can be defined as
\mathsf{KG}^{\prime}(\mathsf{msk},i,x_{i})\coloneqq\mathsf{KG}_{i}(\mathsf{msk},x_{i})
for all
i\in[k-1]
. Finally,
\mathsf{Dec}^{\prime}
is
defined as
\mathsf{Dec}^{\prime}(\mathsf{ct}=(\mathsf{pk},\widetilde{\mathsf{ct}}),\mathsf{sk}_{1},\ldots,\mathsf{sk}_{k-1})\coloneqq\mathsf{Dec}(\mathsf{pk},\widetilde{\mathsf{ct}},\mathsf{sk}_{1},\ldots,\mathsf{sk}_{k-1},\mathsf{sk}_{f})
for the function
f(x_{0},\ldots,x_{k-1})=C_{x_{0}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k-1})
where
C_{x_{0}}
is the circuit
described by ciphertext-attribute
x_{0}
.

Unfortunately,
[
ARYY23
]
only allows for a constant
k
, and
hence we do not currently know of a construction where

k={\mathrm{poly}}(\lambda)
. In the recent work of
[
AKY24
]
, a
variant of MI-ABE (for
k={\mathrm{poly}}(\lambda)
) was achieved from the LWE and evasive LWE assumptions, which is non-standard.
However, the scheme it implies would require the encryption algorithm
\mathsf{Enc}^{\prime}
to utilize the master secret-key

\mathsf{msk}
.

Disucssion on MI-ABE and IO.

We focus on MI-ABE satisfying
Definition

8.1
in this paragraph.
MI-ABE is not stronger than IO since IO is equivalent to multi-input functional encryption (MIFE)
[
GGG+14
]
and MIFE trivially implies MI-ABE.
Although we do not know whether MIFE is separated from MI-ABE, it is likely since PKFE is separated from ABE
[
GMM17
]
and constructing FE schemes is significantly more challenging than ABE schemes.
Moreover, MI-ABE is not currently known from weaker assumptions than IO.
They are qualitatively different primitives, and MI-ABE could be constructed from weaker assumptions in the future.
We also know that MI-ABE implies witness encryption
[
BJK+18
]
, and witness encryption is achieved from the evasive LWE assumption
[
Tsa22
,
VWW22
]
.
Hence, MI-ABE is somewhere between witness encryption and IO.
Achieving MI-ABE from lattice assumptions is an interesting open problem.

8.2
Definitions of ABE-CR
2
-SKL

The syntax of ABE-CR
2
-SKL is defined as follows.

Definition 8.5 (ABE-CR
2
-SKL).

An ABE-CR
2
-SKL scheme
\mathsf{ABE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR^{2}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}
is a tuple of
six algorithms
(\mathsf{Setup},\mathpzc{KG},\mathsf{Enc},\allowbreak\mathpzc{Dec},\mathpzc{Del},\mathsf{Vrfy})
.
Below, let
\mathcal{M}
be the message space of
\mathsf{ABE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR^{2}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}
,
\mathcal{X}
be the
ciphertext-attribute space, and
\mathcal{Y}
the key-attribute space.

\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})\rightarrow(\mathsf{ek},\mathsf{msk})
:

The setup algorithm takes a
security parameter
1^{\lambda}
, and outputs an encryption key

\mathsf{ek}
and a master secret-key
\mathsf{msk}
.

\mathpzc{KG}(\mathsf{msk},y)\rightarrow(\mathpzc{sk},\mathsf{vk})
:

The key generation algorithm takes
the master secret-key
\mathsf{msk}
and a key-attribute
y\in\mathcal{Y}
as inputs, and outputs a decryption key

\mathpzc{sk}
and a verification key
\mathsf{vk}
.

\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{ek},\widetilde{x},\mathsf{m})\rightarrow\mathsf{ct}
:

The encryption algorithm takes an
encryption key
\mathsf{ek}
, a ciphertext-attribute
\widetilde{x}\in\mathcal{X}
, and a message
\mathsf{m}\in\mathcal{M}
as inputs, and outputs a ciphertext
\mathsf{ct}
.

\mathpzc{Dec}(\mathpzc{sk},\mathsf{ct})\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathsf{m}}
:

The decryption
algorithm takes a decryption key
\mathpzc{sk}
and a ciphertext
\mathsf{ct}
,
and outputs a value
\widetilde{\mathsf{m}}
or
\bot
.

\mathpzc{Del}(\mathpzc{sk})\rightarrow\mathsf{cert}
:

The deletion
algorithm takes a decryption key
\mathpzc{sk}
and outputs a deletion
certificate
\mathsf{cert}
.

\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{cert}^{\prime})\rightarrow\top/\bot
:

The verification algorithm
takes a verification key
\mathsf{vk}
and a certificate
\mathsf{cert}^{\prime}
,
and outputs
\top
or
\bot
.

Decryption correctness:

For every
\mathsf{m}\in\mathcal{M}
,
\widetilde{x}\in\mathcal{X}

and
y\in\mathcal{Y}
such that
\widetilde{x}(y)=0
(decryptable), we have

\displaystyle\Pr\left[\mathpzc{Dec}(\mathpzc{sk},\mathsf{ct})\allowbreak=\mathsf{m}\ :\begin{array}[]{ll}(\mathsf{ek},\mathsf{msk})\leftarrow\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})\\
(\mathpzc{sk},\mathsf{vk})\leftarrow\mathpzc{KG}(\mathsf{msk},y)\\
\mathsf{ct}\leftarrow\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{ek},\widetilde{x},\mathsf{m})\end{array}\right]\geq 1-{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

Verification correctness:

For every
y\in\mathcal{Y}
, we have

\displaystyle\Pr\left[\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{cert})=\top\ :\begin{array}[]{ll}(\mathsf{ek},\mathsf{msk})\leftarrow\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})\\
(\mathpzc{sk},\mathsf{vk})\leftarrow\mathpzc{KG}(\mathsf{msk},y)\\
\mathsf{cert}\leftarrow\mathpzc{Del}(\mathpzc{sk})\end{array}\right]\geq 1-{\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda).

Remark
8.6.

We use the same experiment identifier

\mathsf{sel}\textrm{-}\mathsf{ind}\textrm{-}\mathsf{kla}
to refer to
the selective IND-KLA
security experiments of ABE-CR-SKL and
ABE-CR
2
-SKL. This is for the sake of simplicity,
as the exact experiment will be clear from the context.

Selective IND-KLA Security:

This security notion for an
ABE-CR
2
-SKL scheme is defined in the same way as
for ABE-CR-SKL, except that instead of access to the oracle

O_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{Vrfy}}}
in the experiment,
\mathpzc{A}
receives access to the
following oracle:

O_{\mathtt{\mathsf{Vrfy}}}(y,\mathsf{cert})
:

Given
(y,\mathsf{cert})
, it finds an
entry
(y,\mathsf{vk},V)
from
L_{\mathtt{\mathpzc{KG}}}
. (If there is no such entry, it returns
\bot
.)
It then runs
d\coloneqq\mathsf{Vrfy}(\mathsf{vk},\mathsf{cert})
and returns

d
to
\mathpzc{A}
. If
V=\bot
, it updates the entry into

(y,\mathsf{vk},d)
.

8.3
Construction of ABE-CR
2
-SKL from MI-ABE

Construction overview.

We first provide the main idea
behind our ABE-CR
2
-SKL based on MI-ABE, which follows along the lines of our
ABE-CR-SKL construction with some key-differences.
In this case, we directly rely on a BB84-based SKE-CD scheme as a
building block, instead of needing something similar to SKFE-CR-SKL.
Consider now an SKE-CD ciphertext
\mathsf{skecd}.\mathpzc{ct}
of the plaintext
0^{\lambda}
.
Let its corresponding verification-key
\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{vk}
be of the form

\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{vk}=(x,\theta)
, where
x
and
\theta
are
k
-bit strings. Then,
\mathsf{skecd}.\mathpzc{ct}
is
of the form
\ket{\psi_1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\ket{\psi_k}
, where
for
i\in[k]
,
\ket{\psi_i}
is of the following form:

\displaystyle\ket{\psi_i}=\begin{cases}\ket{x[i]}&if~~\theta[i]=0\\
\ket{0}+(-1)^{x[i]}\ket{1}&if~~\theta[i]=1\end{cases}

(74)

Now, for each
i\in\{2,\dots,k\}
and
u\in\{0,1\}
, consider the attribute-key

\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,u}
generated by the MI-ABE key-generation
algorithm for slot
i
and attribute
t\|u
. Here,
t
is a value chosen at
random and is common for all slots corresponding to a given decryption-key

\mathpzc{sk}
. Additionally, for
u\in\{0,1\}
, consider the keys

\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{sk}_{1,u}
corresponding to the attributes
t\|u\|y
where

y
is the actual ABE key-attribute.

Consider now the following state
\rho_{i}
for each
i\in[k]
:

\displaystyle\rho_{i}=\begin{cases}\ket{x[i]}\ket{\abe.\sk_{i,x[i]}}&if~~\theta[i]=0\\
\ket{0}\ket{\abe.\sk_{i,0}}+(-1)^{x[i]}\ket{1}\ket{\abe.\sk_{i,1}}&if~~\theta[i]=1,\end{cases}

(75)

The quantum decryption-key of our scheme will be the tuple
\mathpzc{sk}=(\rho_{i})_{i\in[k]}
. The encryption algorithm is similar to the
ABE-CR-SKL scheme, and outputs an MI-ABE encryption of message
\mathsf{m}

under a policy
C_{\mathsf{ABE}}\|\widetilde{C}
where
\widetilde{C}\leftarrow\mathsf{CC}.\mathsf{Sim}(1^{\lambda})
is a simulator of a compute and compare obfuscator and
C_{\mathsf{ABE}}
is the actual ABE policy.
However, the MI-ABE relation is a little different. The relation is
such that for a set of
k
attributes
y_{1}=t_{1}\|u_{1}\|y
and
y_{2}=t_{2}\|u_{2},\ldots,y_{k}=t_{k}\|u_{k}
, it outputs
0
(decryptable)
whenever
C_{\mathsf{ABE}}(y)=0
(the ABE relation is satisfied) AND
t_{1}=\ldots=t_{k}
AND

\widetilde{C}(u_{1}\|\ldots\|u_{k})=\bot
. Otherwise, it outputs
1
. As in
ABE-CR-SKL, the idea is that
\widetilde{C}
is indistinguishable from
\widetilde{C}^{*}

that is an obfuscation of the compute-and-compare circuit

\mathbf{CC}[D[\mathsf{lock}\oplus r,\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk}],\mathsf{lock},0]
. Here,
\mathsf{lock}
and
r
are
random values, and
\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk}
is the secret-key of the SKE-CD scheme. The
circuit
D
is such that
D(u)
outputs
\mathsf{lock}\oplus r\oplus\mathsf{SKECD}.\mathsf{CDec}(\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk},u)
, where
\mathsf{SKECD}.\mathsf{CDec}
is the classical
decryption algorithm of the SKE-CD scheme.

Consequently, when every
\mathpzc{sk}=(\rho_{i})_{i\in[k]}
the adversary
receives is generated using an SKE-CD encryption of
r
(instead of

0^{\lambda}
as in the scheme), then any tuple of
k
MI-ABE keys of the
adversary having attributes
\big(t_{1}\|u_{1}\|y,t_{2}\|u_{2}\ldots,t_{k}\|u_{k}\big)

satisfies one of the three conditions:

•

The values
t_{1}\ldots t_{k}
are not all the same.

•

C_{\mathsf{ABE}}(y)\neq 0
.

•

For
u=u_{1}\|\ldots\|u_{k}
,
D[\mathsf{lock}\oplus r,\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk}](u)

returns
\mathsf{lock}
.

Notice that the former condition ensures that the adversary cannot
interleave keys corresponding to different decryption-keys.
The last condition holds because the adversary never receives

\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,1-x[i]}
for any
i
such that
\theta[i]=0
.
Consequently,
u
and
x
are the same at all positions where

\theta[i]=0
. This means that
\mathsf{SKECD}.\mathsf{CDec}(\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk},u)
outputs
r
by the
classical decryption property of
\mathsf{SKECD}
(Definition
3.12
).
It is important to note that the positions
i
where
\theta[i]=1

(the Hadamard positions) have no effect on the value output by

\mathsf{SKECD}.\mathsf{CDec}

as their purpose is just in the verification of deletion.
As a result, the security of
MI-ABE allows to simulate the adversary’s view in this hybrid, as
no “decryptable” set of
k
keys is given out.

Importantly, the switch from SKE-CD encryptions of
0^{\lambda}
to
r
is
indistinguishable, given that the adversary deletes all the information
in the SKE-CD ciphertexts. To enforce this, the deletion algorithm
requires the adversary to measure both the SKE-CD and MI-ABE registers
for each slot to obtain values
(c_{i},d_{i})_{i\in[k]}
. Then, given
the values
\{\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,0}\oplus\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,1}\}_{i\in[k]}
as
part of the verification key, the verification checks whether
x[i]=c_{i}\oplus d_{i}\cdot(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,0}\oplus\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,1})
holds for
every
i\in[k]
such that
\theta[i]=1
. As a result, we are able
use a standard hybrid argument to turn any distinguisher (of the

0^{\lambda}
and
r
hybrids) into an attack on the certified deletion
security of the SKE-CD scheme.

Construction.

We will construct an ABE-CR
2
-SKL
scheme
\mathsf{ABE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR^{2}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}=\mathsf{ABE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR^{2}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.(\mathsf{Setup},\mathpzc{KG},\mathsf{Enc},

\mathpzc{Dec},\mathpzc{Del},\mathsf{Vrfy})
using the following building blocks:

•

Multi-Input (Ciphertext-Policy) ABE Scheme
\mathsf{MI}\textrm{-}\mathsf{ABE}=\mathsf{MI}\textrm{-}\mathsf{ABE}.(\mathsf{Setup},\mathsf{KG},\allowbreak\mathsf{Enc},\mathsf{Dec})
for the following relation:

R(\widetilde{x}\|z,y_{1},\cdots,y_{k})
:

Let
\widetilde{x}
be
interpreted as a circuit and
z
as a
k
-input circuit.

–

Parse
y_{1}=t_{1}\|u_{1}\|y
.

–

Parse
y_{i}=t_{i}\|u_{i}
for every
i\in 2,\ldots,k
.

–

If
t_{i}\neq t_{j}
for some
i,j\in[k]
, output
1
. Otherwise, go to the next step.

–

If
z(u_{1}\|\cdots\|u_{k})=\bot
AND
\widetilde{x}(y)=0
, output
0

(decryptable). Else, output
1
.

•

Compute-and-Compare Obfuscation
\mathsf{CC}.\mathsf{Obf}
with the simulator

\mathsf{CC}.\mathsf{Sim}
.

•

BB84-based SKE-CD scheme
\mathsf{SKECD}=\mathsf{SKECD}.(\mathsf{KG},\mathpzc{Enc},\mathpzc{Dec},\mathpzc{Del},\mathsf{Vrfy})
with the classical
decryption algorithm
\mathsf{SKECD}.\mathsf{CDec}
.

The description of each algorithm of
\mathsf{ABE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR^{2}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}
is as follows.

\mathsf{ABE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR^{2}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
:

•

Generate
(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk},\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{msk})\leftarrow\mathsf{MI}\textrm{-}\mathsf{ABE}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
.

•

Generate
\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk}\leftarrow\mathsf{SKECD}.\mathsf{KG}(1^{\lambda})
.

•

Output
\mathsf{ek}\coloneqq\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk}
and

\mathsf{msk}\coloneqq(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{msk},\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk})
.

\mathsf{ABE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR^{2}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{KG}(\mathsf{msk},y)
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{msk}=(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{msk},\mathsf{ske}.\mathsf{msk})
.

•

Sample a random value
t\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{\lambda}
.

•

Generate

(\mathsf{skecd}.\mathpzc{ct},\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{vk})\leftarrow\mathsf{SKECD}.\mathpzc{Enc}(\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{sk},0^{\lambda})
.

\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{vk}
is of the form

(x,\theta)\in\{0,1\}^{k}\times\{0,1\}^{k}
, and
\mathsf{skecd}.\mathpzc{ct}
is of
the form

\ket{\psi_1}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{1}}}}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\ket{\psi_{k}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{k}}}}}
.

•

Generate
\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{sk}_{1,b}\leftarrow\mathsf{MI}\textrm{-}\mathsf{ABE}.\mathsf{KG}(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{msk},1,t\|b\|y)

for each
b\in\{0,1\}
.

•

For every
i\in 2,\ldots,k
, do the following:

–

Generate
\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,b}\leftarrow\mathsf{MI}\textrm{-}\mathsf{ABE}.\mathsf{KG}(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{msk},i,t\|b)

for each
b\in\{0,1\}
.

•

For every
i\in[k]
, do the following:

–

Prepare a register
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{i}}}}
that is initialized
to
\ket{0\cdots 0}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{i}}}}}
.

–

Apply the map

\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{i}}}}}\ket{v}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{i}}}}}\rightarrow\ket{u}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{i}}}}}\ket{v\oplus\abe.\sk_{i,u}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{i}}}}}

to the registers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{SKECD.CT_{i}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}{\mathsf{ABE.SK_{i}}}}
, and
obtain the resulting state
\rho_{i}
.

•

Output
\mathpzc{sk}:=(\rho_{i})_{i\in[k]}
and

\mathsf{vk}\coloneqq\big(\mathsf{skecd}.\mathsf{vk},\{\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,0}\oplus\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{sk}_{i,1}\}_{i\in[k]:\theta[i]=1}\big)
.

\mathsf{ABE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR^{2}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{ek},\widetilde{x},\mathsf{m})
:

•

Parse
\mathsf{ek}=\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk}
.

•

Generate
\widetilde{C}\leftarrow\mathsf{CC}.\mathsf{Sim}(1^{\lambda},\mathsf{pp}_{D},1)
, where
\mathsf{pp}_{D}
consists of circuit parameters of
D
defined in the security proof.

•

Generate
\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{ct}\leftarrow\mathsf{MI}\textrm{-}\mathsf{ABE}.\mathsf{Enc}(\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{pk},\widetilde{x}\|\widetilde{C},\mathsf{m})
.

•

Output
\mathsf{ct}\coloneqq\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{ct}
.

\mathsf{ABE}\textrm{-}\mathsf{CR^{2}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SKL}.\mathpzc{Dec}(\mathpzc{sk},\mathsf{ct})
:

•

Parse
\mathpzc{sk}=(\rho_{i})_{i\in[k]}
and
\mathsf{ct}=\mathsf{abe}.\mathsf{ct}
. We
denote the register holding
\rho_{i}
as
